# Тема: игровой-дизайн

Дословные слова авторов по теме «игровой-дизайн»: 293 фрагментов из 88 роликов. Читай подряд — это опыт практиков в их собственных словах; «Мысль» и «Для Напарника» — вывод разметчика.



# 🎬 "He Built a Game For His Girlfriend — The New York Times Paid Millions" — Brain & Behavior Explained


### Один пазл в день: дефицит, три минуты и сложность на 4-6% выше навыка  ·  [05:11](https://youtu.be/nsg9FZucQts?t=311)
**Мысль:** Ограничение до одного пазла в сутки сделало игру ритуалом; обратная связь мгновенная, сложность в потоке (4-6% выше навыка), а счётчики серий делают пропуск дорогим.

> But wait, sharing alone doesn't explain why people kept coming back day after day. Most successful apps want you scrolling endlessly, right? They want infinite content. Well, Wordle intentionally broke that golden rule, relying instead on the scarcity effect. This is the psychological principle where we consistently assign a higher perceived value to things that are limited in availability by restricting players to just one identical puzzle a day. For basically 3 minutes, Wordle transformed a standard word game into a highly anticipated global ritual. Inside that tiny 3minute window, it delivered these perfectly calibrated dopamine hits. Every single guess gives you instant visual feedback. The unpredictable flip of those gray, yellow, and green tiles that directly mirrors BF Skinner's variable reward patterns. It's kind of like a slot machine. Plus, the difficulty sits right in a psychological flow state. It requires just 4 to 6% more skill than your current ability, making it super satisfying instead of frustrating. Add in those double-digit street counters, and the psychological cost of skipping even one day becomes incredibly high.

**Для Напарника:** Короткий ритуал (одна сессия-ритуал в день как мягкий якорь), сложность шага чуть выше текущего. Часть про серии не применима: стрик-давление противоречит тону.



# 🎬 23 Ways to Nudge: A Review of Technology-Mediated Nudging in Human-Computer Interaction — ACM SIGCHI


### Определение нуджа и пример с кассой: поставить полезное на уровень глаз без запретов  ·  [01:45](https://youtu.be/-U3Bo7KR108?t=105)
**Мысль:** Нудж меняет архитектуру выбора предсказуемо, не запрещая вариантов и не меняя стимулов; фрукты на уровне глаз вместо снеков на кассе.

> And it described any aspect of the choice architecture that can alters people behavior in a predictable way without forbidding any option or changing their incentives so leveraging the concept of nudging researchers have designed it small changes in the way that choices and information are presented with the goal or guiding to decide choices so for instance one of the most popular nigel's has been introduced in a supermarket so usually when you go to the cash register you have like a bus impulse and they put there to locate their very unhealthy items such as cake and snacks and what they did was to replace these unhealthy snacks by fruit and other healthy items and they found that while their post options were still available like cake and fruit just positioning the healthy item nearby at eye level it was enough to motivate people to adopt healthy options.

**Для Напарника:** Выбор без выбора: порядок и расположение вариантов в пикере длительности и шага (легче всего выбрать полезное) без запретов.



# 🎬 5 Game Design Secrets That Fix Retention & LTV — Kutovaya Elena


### Три вложенных цикла: сессия, прогрессия, мета; что происходит между сессиями  ·  [06:08](https://youtu.be/EAfYEhCve6Q?t=368)
**Мысль:** Ретеншн падает не потому, что продукт плох, а потому что нет второго и третьего циклов. Вопрос: что происходит, пока пользователя нет в приложении?

> Engagement loops. The core loop is the simplest kind of engagement loop. It's the smallest step of actions a user repeats over and over. Action result, new reason to act again. In games, this gets designed first before anything else. Because DA and retention depend on it directly. Game design usually has at least three loops nested inside each other. The session loop what happens in one visit over a few minutes. The progression loop, what happens between visits over hours or days. usually about building something up. This is what drives your day seven and day 30 retention. The metal loop, the third one, long-term goals over weeks or months, usually bigger goals than any single action. This is what drives long-term LTV. Here is what a hot product could borrow. Don't stop at the session loop. Build more depth. Give the user a reason to come back tomorrow. That reason is exactly what shows up as retention on your dashboard. Dow and retention don't drop because your product is bad. They drop because the second and the third loops are missing and almost nobody will. Here is a good question for any product. What happens between sessions while your user is not in the app? If their answer is nothing, no buildup, no progress, nothing to look forward to, done the loop race at the very first level, no amount of push notifications, you'll get retention past occasional and LTV plateaus right along with it.

**Для Напарника:** Остров должен «жить» между сессиями (небо по часу суток, очаг, существо у костра) и давать повод вернуться завтра без пушей.



# 🎬 ADHD & Time Blindness — Russell Barkley, PhD - Dedicated to ADHD Science+


### Событие-реакция-исход: пока разрыв короткий, лобная кора не нужна  ·  [09:19](https://youtu.be/IHgbNGPZphc?t=559)
**Мысль:** Если событие, реакция и последствие следуют вплотную (как в видеоигре), СДВГ не мешает; как только между ними вставлен временной интервал, нужна функция «связывания времени» лобных долей, которую СДВГ-мозг тянет хуже. Вот почему игры держат часами, а отложенные задачи — нет.

> The way we look at situations and tasks is going to be important we need to think about tasks and situations as coming to us as what I call ero Arrangements events things coming toward us responses how we prepare for the things that are coming at us in time and consequences the outcomes how do we do were we ready on time or not so ero Arrangements now here's the thing about ADHD right if these ero arrangements are together very closely there's hardly any time between them it's kind of like a video game right then guess what no problem you don't need a frontal lobe to deal with event response outcome Arrangements many organisms can do this particularly animals can do this but other organisms can as well they are schenerian creatures like BF Skinner they respond to stimulus response stimulus response right so if these arrangements are tight together in time your frontal lobe isn't necessary and we can do them easily this is why people with ADHD can play video games for hours and do so effectively because the video game has arranged this kind of very addictive schedule of consequences for engaging with the task and with the environment but the minute you insert time between any of these three parts the minute you insert an interval between the event and the response required for the event or between my response and finding out how I did the outcome you need a frontal lobe because as Joaquin Foster said after studying the frontal lobes of primates all of his career if we can reduce the functions of the frontal lobe in the executive brain to one essential idea it is The Binding of events over time in mind so as to deal with the future we are a Time binding animal nobody no other species does it the way we do so if you put time in these Arrangements you are going to disable the person with ADHD.

**Для Напарника:** Главный принцип для Напарника: сократить задержку между действием и наградой. Каждый шаг сессии должен получать немедленный видимый отклик (рост острова, реакция кота), а не отложенный итог в конце дня.



# 🎬 ADHD and Motivation — How to ADHD


### Что мотивирует СДВГ: срочное, новое, интересное  ·  [01:57](https://youtu.be/OM0Xv0eVGtY?t=117)
**Мысль:** Мозг СДВГ плохо справляется с длинным, повторяющимся и скучным; зажигают срочность, новизна и личный интерес; игры дают это по умолчанию.

> According to research, ADHD brains tend to have trouble with tasks that are lengthy, repetitive, or boring. On the flip side, there are a few things that give ADHD brains a bunch of motivational planks: Things that are urgent. Things that are new, or novel And things that are of personal interest. In other words, things that are stimulating, or engaging to our brains. Which is also why stimulant medication and exercise can be helpful. This need for stimulation isn’t a choice, it’s how our brains work. Some tasks naturally include these motivational planks -- Video games, anyone? Which is why they hold our focus so well. But for those that don’t, we can add them.

**Для Напарника:** Для каждой механики Напарника проверять, даёт ли она срочность, новизну или интерес; недостающее добавлять.

# 🎬 Animal Crossing is Lost — Croey!


### Жизнь мира независимо от игрока делала город живым  ·  [00:30](https://youtu.be/vOhB7KcidmE?t=30)
**Мысль:** В ранних Animal Crossing жители жили своей жизнью, магазины открывались, сезоны шли, а контроль игрока был ограничен намеренно, поэтому город ощущался местом, а не проектом.

> Weakness, it was the reason the world felt alive. Villagers living their day-to-day lives, shops opening and closing, seasons passing in real time. Life moved on regardless if you were there or not. All things that are still core parts of the franchise to this day, but there was one crucial distinction. >> >> You see, back then, you weren't the architect of your town. You were just another resident. Your control over the world was limited, and that limitation was intentional. If you didn't like where the rivers were placed, sorry, bud, you were out of luck. You couldn't terraform the land around you. The town you moved into was the town you got, and you were happy with that. The town felt like an actual place and not some project or canvas.

**Для Напарника:** Остров и кот должны жить и своим ритмом (время суток, кот занят своим), а не только по нажатию игрока.


### Полный контроль изменил фантазию: место для жизни стало объектом для совершенствования  ·  [02:19](https://youtu.be/vOhB7KcidmE?t=139)
**Мысль:** Когда игрок проектирует всё, среда подстраивается под него, а острова превращаются в выставочные кураторские витрины для соцсетей.

> You had full control over your world. And while that freedom wasn't necessarily a bad thing, it did fundamentally change the fantasy. There was a dramatic frame shift. No longer were you moving into a living town, you were now designing the perfect town. Players stopped adapting and living in the environment, and now the environment was the one doing the adapting. At this point, Animal Crossing quietly stopped being a simulation and instead started becoming a creative sandbox. What did players do with that new found freedom? Well, they went the way of social media, of course. Islands became highly curated spaces, aesthetic builds, perfectly designed environments meant to be shared and showcased. Islands were now becoming centerpieces on Instagram and Twitter posts. No longer were islands a place to live, they were something to perfect.

**Для Напарника:** Остров Напарника растёт от усилий, а не конструируется; не превращать его в объект идеального оформления и сравнения с другими.


### Иллюзия жизни: независимые персонажи, странности, которые ты не проектировал  ·  [03:53](https://youtu.be/vOhB7KcidmE?t=233)
**Мысль:** Жители имели свои жизни, а город имел собственные причуды и личность; ты адаптировался к нему, как новичок в школе.

> The thing that made earlier Animal Crossing towns feel so alive wasn't just the villagers or the seasons changing, it was the fact that the world was a living, breathing thing that existed outside of the player's control. Villagers had their lives that didn't necessarily revolve around the player. The layout of the town had quirks that you didn't design. The town itself had its own identity, and as a resident of said town, you had to adapt to it. It felt like being the new kid in school. That independence created the illusion of life.

**Для Напарника:** У кота и жителей острова должны быть свои причуды и предпочтения, не зависящие от игрока.


### Контроль над всем превращает место в проект  ·  [04:37](https://youtu.be/vOhB7KcidmE?t=277)
**Мысль:** Когда мир не сопротивляется игроку, вместо открытия характера места игрок создаёт его, и мир становится проектом.

> But when the player controls almost everything about the environment, that relationship starts to break down. In New Horizons, being able to reshape the terrain and decide where rivers go and where the cliffs go, the world no longer pushes back against the player in the same way that it once did. Instead of discovering the character of the town, players are now tasked to create the character of the town. Naturally, that changes how people approach the game. While that level of creative freedom opened up entirely new possibilities for the series, it also had an unintentional side effect. When the player controls everything about the town, the world, the island around them, the world becomes less of a place and more of a project.

**Для Напарника:** Оставить в острове элементы, которых игрок не выбирал (находки, подарки кота), чтобы мир удивлял.


### Чем больше контроля, тем труднее миру казаться самостоятельным  ·  [06:38](https://youtu.be/vOhB7KcidmE?t=398)
**Мысль:** Задача в том, чтобы мир оставался живым там, где игрок контролирует всё.

> The more control the player has over the world, the harder it becomes for that world to feel like it exists as its own entity. So, the real challenge for the next Animal Crossing might not be bigger islands or more furniture. It might be figuring out how to make the world feel alive again.

**Для Напарника:** Баланс: игрок влияет на остров своими сессиями, но реакции мира и кота остаются непредсказуемыми и тёплыми.



# 🎬 Become A Better Workshop FACILITATOR In 8 Minutes (Facilitation Technique) — AJ&Smart


### Эффект края и Peak-End: запоминают старт, пик и финал  ·  [00:49](https://youtu.be/So8aseCO3hY?t=49)
**Мысль:** Люди помнят первые и последние элементы серии и один пик, а не все моменты; если старт и конец сильные, покрыто около 80% запоминаемого.

> Law number three start strong and stronger this is one of the easiest laws to implement but also the law that 90 percent of workshoppers forget this law relies on two phenomena of the human brain one is called the serial position effect and the serial position effect basically means that people tend to remember the first few things and the last few things in a series this can be numbers this can be events this can be a whole day at disneyland they generally remember the start and the end more than anything else that's the first phenomena and the second phenomena is called the peak end rule and the peak end rule basically says that people don't remember an experience based on all of the individual moments of an experience so for example they won't remember the workshop because of all the individual moments they'll remember one peak meaning one high point and they'll remember the end and pretty much nothing else two of these things combined mean that if you get the start right and if you get the end right you've basically covered eighty percent of the things that people are actually going to remember about your workshop and this is why at aj and smart we try to make sure our workshops start strong start memorably and they end strong because they're going to forget everything in the middle anyway and really the start and the end are the way you can really build in powerful moments that people go away with and think about forever so to repeat start strong and stronger this literally means the ending is the most important part of your workshop the starts of course important but even if you manage to mess that up you need to stick the ending people will leave feeling like was the most amazing thing they've ever been part of.

**Для Напарника:** Сессия Фокуса: сильный старт (starting-хореография) и пик на финале (RevealIsland); середину можно держать тихой.



# 🎬 Body Doubling Will Change How You Work Forever — FLOWN


### Три уровня давления: тихая компания, средний (цель и итог), высокий (фасилитатор)  ·  [04:51](https://youtu.be/8F88hppZllg?t=291)
**Мысль:** Регулировать три параметра: взаимодействие, структуру, видимость обязательства; подбирать по сложности задачи и уровню энергии.

> Think of it as a dial from silent companionship on one end to fully structured sessions on the other. You're adjusting three things how much you interact with the other person, how much structure wraps around the session, and how visible your commitment is. So what does that look like in practice. Especially for virtual body doubling. Low pressure camera on. No talking, no set time limit, no check in. You're just there together. Think of it like working in a library. Nobody's watching you. But the quiet presence of others keeps you anchored. You less likely to drift off to your phone or disappear into a YouTube rabbit hole. These are good for admin, repetitive work, creative thinking, or maybe low energy days. You're adding just enough presence to prevent the drift. Medium pressure camera on a clear start time, you state what you're working on either out loud or in the chat. There's a light checking at the end. How did that go? Did you get it done? This is where it starts to feel noticeably different from working alone. You've made a small public commitment. Someone else knows what you're working on, and that's enough social energy to move forward without tipping into stress. This is good for your day to day work tasks you've been avoiding, anything that keeps carrying over on your to do list. And finally, high pressure camera on a professional facilitator sets the pace. You state a clear specific goal in the session is time boxed? Maybe 1 hour or 2 hours. At the end, you report back, did I finish what got in the way? This is the most powerful tier. It works brilliantly for tasks where you're truly stuck, where you need short bursts of activation, or for that project you've been circling around for a week. This is great for high resistant tasks. Deadline crunches, or breaking through task paralysis. Before you sit down to work, ask yourself two questions. How hard is this task and how much energy do I have today? Over time. You learn what type of body works best for you, so you can match the intensity to the task.

**Для Напарника:** Режимы Фокуса: «просто рядом», «скажу цель и отчитаюсь», «жёсткий таймбокс»; перед стартом два вопроса: насколько тяжело и сколько сил.

> So the first thing I want to explain is the difference between an activity loop and a game loop right a lot of people are not aware of that so earlier if you have been in the community for long enough you'll know we've done we did a food design food hero design challenge where we go in and we design how to make kids eat healthily in China and different countries and so we work with a client and then we released it to the public and a lot of people have good designs and one of the things that we often see for people's designs like even if they're good they have something we called an activity loop most almost all systems have to a collectivity loop right and so we'll see here like oh the stay starts here you know we starts doing step one and then we do step two so you see teacher checks the food journals you get one power card but the blood right and then you do each of these steps and at the end it says eat at least one healthy food immersed in the food journal that's the last step and then goes back to the next day the beginning of the day now this is an activity loop because this is the loop you want people do over and over and over and over again right however it is not what I consider a game loop because the last step doesn't necessarily empower the first step and maybe my definition is different others out there I believe my definition is more useful to creating better designs so bear with me on that usually the last step is I'll complete the whole process right you know and sometimes like finish a module right and then of course to loop back people should start the module another module again right the thing is that the last step does not empower the first step it ends and you're hoping people come back and be the first step right you want people to do the activity but they're not creating some kind of game loop where we're the last that empowers the first step and the whole thing is interesting.

# 🎬 Core Game Loops — Timothy Cain


### Core loop: самые частые повторяющиеся действия большинства  ·  [01:17](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=77)
**Мысль:** Core loop — набор действий, которым большинство игроков занимается большую часть времени.

> The core game Loop is the set of actions in a game that most players will spend most of their time doing you can think of it as what are the most frequent and repeated actions that players take in your game.

**Для Напарника:** Чётко определить core loop Напарника (выбрать шаг, запустить Фокус, получить отклик) и вкладывать главное внимание в него.


### Как определить core loop: час игры, демо, стример  ·  [05:24](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=324)
**Мысль:** Достаточно часа игры или просмотра стримера, чтобы увидеть, что человек делает чаще всего; у игр без чёткого core loop человек не понимает, что происходит. Демо и vertical slice должны показывать core loop, а не просто красивую картинку.

> Don't have to play an entire game from start to finish to know what its core game Loop is you usually you can pick up a game play it for an hour and you can probably pretty accurately Define its core game Loop if it's a good game and this is what why some games fail I think is because they don't have a well defined core game Loop and you can play for an hour and you're like I still don't know what's going on alternatively you could just play a demo the whole point of a demo is to express the core game Loop by the way if you're if you're making a vertical slice you should also be thinking core game Loop because that's what you're trying to express not just here's a visual example of the game but I've got enough of the core game Loop in there that you understand what the game's going to be like when you play it's not enough vertical slices you just it's not enough for them just to be pretty they have to instantiate the core game Loop a lot of times I've noticed that if you just watch a streamer play the game that's enough for you to figure out the core game Loop they just watch them for an hour and you see what they're doing over and over and over again are they jumping are they sneaking around are they hacking terminals what seems to be the thing that they do the most that's the core game Loop.

**Для Напарника:** Проверка Напарника: за час наблюдения за новым пользователем должно быть очевидно, что он делает чаще всего, и это должно быть приятно.


### Core loop и дизайн-столпы — разные вещи  ·  [06:38](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=398)
**Мысль:** Столпы объясняют, как каждая фича служит замыслу; core loop — те из фич, которые игроки используют чаще всего. Core loop должен касаться всех столпов, остальные фичи могут их не затрагивать.

> Let me make a big distinguish here between core game loops and design pillars I have a whole video on design pillars and I'll link it below they are not the same thing the design pillars are there to explain how every feature that you've selected to be in the game serves those pillars but a core game Loop is you saying okay of all those features that we've put in the game that serve the design pillars these are the ones I expect most of the players to experience most of the time and there's difference there because you may have things that you put in like let's say you put in reactivity that's something I love putting in my game my games reactivity might be a design pillar which means when you put in features your you're going to always ask the question of how does the player what the player does when they interact with this feature come back to them how are they being told hey here's how good you're doing with this thing the core game Loop would be those features that the player does a lot you would expect all of them to have large amounts of reactivity because if this is what the player is doing most of the time it better be supporting that design pillar of reactivity because I've talked about you can occasionally have a feature that doesn't support all the design pillars like you may put something in that's if humor is one of your design pillars you may have something in the game that's not that funny as long as overall the game is funny but for your core game Loop it should probably touch on all your pillars because it's these features are what your the player is doing a lot it should it should re it should have a basis in those design pillars.

**Для Напарника:** Ввести столпы Напарника (тепло, без стыда, дофамин) и проверять, что core loop затрагивает каждый из них.



# 🎬 Cozy Games Need an Edge — auranym


### Уют — пространство «просто существовать»  ·  [01:02](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=62)
**Мысль:** Animal Crossing давал место, где можно существовать без необходимости думать или тревожиться, участвуя в механиках сколько хочется.

> It was a space where I could just enjoy existing without having to think or worry about anything else and could engage with the mechanics of the game as much or as little as I wanted to. I think that is where cozy games in general really shine.

**Для Напарника:** Дом и Мир как место присутствия, где можно ничего не делать и всё равно быть в порядке.


### Безопасность при сохранённой возможности учиться  ·  [01:21](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=81)
**Мысль:** Уют — ощущение безопасного и удобного мира, где ещё есть чему научиться. В дизайне это баланс между свободой игрока и намеренными ограничениями.

> The feeling of living in a world where I can feel safe and comfortable, but where I still have a lot to learn. From a game design perspective, there's a push and pull between player agency and intentional restrictions. And it seems like it's pretty difficult to get right.

**Для Напарника:** Баланс для Напарника: безопасный предсказуемый мир плюс ощущение, что есть куда расти.


### Уютной игре нужен «край» — доля дискомфорта для контраста  ·  [02:09](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=129)
**Мысль:** Немного холодного и резкого делает мягкое тёплое одеяло остальной игры значимым и уютным.

> To me, what a cozy game needs to feel fulfilling is something that gives the game a bit of discomfort, which lets the comfort of the rest of the game feel meaningful. Something sharp, cold. Something that makes the soft, warm blanket of the rest of the game feel comforting and well, cozy. A cozy game needs an edge.

**Для Напарника:** Допустить в тоне Напарника лёгкую иронию или честную каплю несовершенства для контраста (но не стыд и не давление).


### Три критерия уютной игры  ·  [03:21](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=201)
**Мысль:** Простые и понятные механики, медленный методичный геймплей, намеренно заложенное ощущение комфорта.

> Feel free to disagree with these, but to me, a cozy game needs to fit three criteria. One, it has mechanics that are simple and easy to grasp. Two, it has slow, methodical gameplay. Three, the feeling of comfort is common and intended by the developers.

**Для Напарника:** Рабочая проверка для любой новой фичи Напарника по трём критериям: просто, медленно, намеренно тепло.


### Медленное не должно тратить время игрока  ·  [07:29](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=449)
**Мысль:** В Animal Crossing лишние шаги (перетаскивание инструментов, продажа по одному, сложное сохранение) раздражают; игра должна ценить, что человек выбрал играть, а не заняться другим.

> Feel that some of the gameplay was out of date. I mean, yeah, it is out of date. The game is 25 years old. But what I mean is that the gameplay is at a snail's pace at times. Equipping your tools by dragging them onto your character, selling items one at a time, even saving the game is a whole process. But maybe this was a consequence of the times. Maybe there were technical limitations and concessions had to be made with some of the quality of life features. Well, that's partially true, but there were actually similar complaints about the latest entry in the franchise, Animal Crossing: New Horizons. I remember people complaining a lot about the lack of bulk crafting and about tool durability and the fact that the game feels like a lot of its mechanics are designed with the goal of wasting your time. Like I mentioned in my first video about Silksong, I personally think that a game should always try to value the fact that a player is choosing to play it instead of doing something else. So I think that the fact that even 20 years later, the Animal Crossing franchise is still struggling with this aspect means that it's a good case study of how slow, methodical gameplay can veer too far into point two, where it starts to feel like a waste of time.

**Для Напарника:** Любое действие в Напарнике (старт сессии, отметка шага) должно занимать минимум тапов; медленность только в атмосфере, не в интерфейсе.


### Хорошая медлительность — в действиях, не в меню  ·  [08:51](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=531)
**Мысль:** Ловля рыбы, перестановка мебели, разговоры: мелкие мгновенные действия с понятной обратной связью, без трения, где понятно, удалось ли действие.

> And despite all of the negativity I've expressed so far, I do think there are examples of Animal Crossing being very, very good at slow gameplay. I find that the acts of catching fish and bugs, talking to your neighbors, placing and rearranging furniture, and more recently, terraforming are all really well designed. What sets these apart from my earlier complaints though is that these are all part of truly playing the game and not part of tedious menuing. Let's go back to our cozy game framework, to point one. Simple, easy to grasp mechanics. All of Animal Crossing's slow, methodical tasks are performed through tiny microscopic gameplay mechanics that are both grounded in reality and simplified to a degree that feedback is immediate, understandable, and satisfying. Swinging a net and pushing a sofa are instantaneous actions, so there's no friction in performing the action, and it's always clear when you succeeded or how and why you didn't. The onus is on the player to use these small scale actions how they see fit, which respects the player's time and choices to live in the game's world at their own pace.

**Для Напарника:** Все внутренние действия острова и кота делать мгновенными с чётким откликом; меню и подтверждения свести к минимуму.


### Отсутствие контроля делает мир живым  ·  [10:10](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=610)
**Мысль:** Нельзя выбирать планировку города или кто переедет: ощущение, что мир живёт с тобой и без тебя, и ты лишь часть его.

> There are plenty of things that can happen that are completely outside of the player's control. There's no control over the town layout, the villagers moving in or away, even what your character looks like. It's abrasive, and some could say it doesn't respect the player. But I personally find that the opposite is true. While it can take this abrasiveness a bit far at times, I think the lack of control adds to the feeling that you're living in a world that's alive. You have agency over yourself, and you choose how to live in a world that moves around you, in a world that lives and breathes with or without you, and where you're just a part of it. That is the magic of Animal Crossing to me. making the most of what you have, the neighbors you meet, the furniture you find, the time of day and year. Living in a town where you participate, can live in, but not dictate.

**Для Напарника:** Остров может сам удивлять небольшими событиями (редкая находка, гость у костра), которые игрок не контролирует, но принимает.


### Неидеальный мир, который ты улучшаешь, делает уют значимым  ·  [13:16](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=796)
**Мысль:** Мусор на карте и свалка в Animal Crossing и Stardew создают чувство, что мир далёк от совершенства, а игрок на нём что-то меняет; город стартует с низкой точки.

> It's a pretty scrappy experience, honestly. And it helps sell the feeling that you're just barely getting by despite the coziness. I really think that the scrappiness, though, helps sell the coziness. It makes it feel more real and meaningful. Stardew Valley is pretty filthy as well, at least at the start of the game. There's trash found everywhere, like in Animal Crossing, especially the southernmost point of Cindersap Forest where the sewer drips green sludge into the ocean. And you can also find old CDs manufactured by Joja all over the map, the same company that's trying to turn the community center into a warehouse. All of this again adds to the feeling that the world is far from perfect and adds to the feeling that you, as the new farmer, are doing something about it. It adds some depth by establishing the town at a low point.

**Для Напарника:** Остров стартует «бедным и пустым» (без стыда), а рост видимо улучшает мир: так прогресс виден контрастом.


### Смена тона очищает вкус и сохраняет свежесть тепла  ·  [14:21](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=861)
**Мысль:** Если механики и арт зафиксированы, разнообразие тона (явное в диалогах, неявное в мире) создаёт контраст, который удерживает уют свежим.

> The point is, I think that if mechanics and art style are locked in, then a game can always add some variety by mixing up its tone every now and then. Whether it's explicit through the dialogue or implicit like the world itself, it adds some contrast that helps cleanse the palette to keep the warmth, the comfort, and the coziness feeling fresher for longer.

**Для Напарника:** Разнообразить реплики и сцены Напарника (рассветы, дождь, ночь), чтобы тепло не приедалось.



# 🎬 Creating Our Duolingo Characters • Greg Hartman • Duocon 2020 — Duolingo


### Персонаж как «мысленный мяч»: меньше трудности, больше цели  ·  [01:33](https://youtu.be/m-3-D7S0piw?t=93)
**Мысль:** Аналогия со спортом: с мячом и соревнованием думаешь меньше о физическом усилии. Персонаж и мир делают долгий трудный путь приятным.

> One of the biggest reasons we're adding characters is because we believe characters can play a large role in motivating people. A lot of games we play internally have great characters and those characters help to keep us engaged. At Duolingo we love the benefits that games have on our brain health. I think of this the same way sports benefit our physical health. Sometimes it's hard to just get up and run around your yard, but once you introduce a ball and healthy competition, you think less about how physically hard it is, and more about the ball and the goal. Because let's face it, language journeys can sometimes be long and difficult. So Duolingo strives to introduce that mental ball to help keep you motivated. And characters can help with that, by dropping an audience into a world that they enjoy spending time in.

**Для Напарника:** Кот и остров как «мяч» сессии Фокус: внимание переключается с усилия на присутствие напарника и рост острова.

> Another way to look at this is, if a developer focuses on making a game as compelling as it possibly can be, and it ends up really fun, interesting and makes players want to play it again and again, then that developer won't need to rely on manipulative psychological tricks to artificially improve their metrics.

# 🎬 Designing Addiction: The Twisted Psychology Of Game Design — goukigod


### Ошибка игрока и «защита от невезения» подкармливают веру в неизбежный выигрыш  ·  [07:37](https://youtu.be/K0M1PuQaE8s?t=457)
**Мысль:** Показ шансов не спасает: время и переменные неизвестны, а гарантия раз в 30 паков укрепляет иллюзию, что выигрыш близок.

> Another trait among problem gamblers that slot machines exploit is gamblers fallacy this is where the gambler believes that if something happens more often than not during a specific time period that the opposite is therefore more likely to happen in the future so for example if someone had been playing on a machine without a win for a long time it might seem logical that a big win will eventually happen in the crackdown on loot boxes some countries have demanded the game to display the odds of winning in a bid to warn players of the risk apex legends has even gone as far as displaying the odds in game in theory this level of transparency is fairer because it tells you exactly what your chances of winning are so how can this possibly be a bad thing the problem with knowing the odds is that you might assume that you can predict when a win is likely to happen let's say you know that there's a 20% chance of winning according to the odds you should win every 5 turns or less except that's not exactly how it works unless the rules state that a win is guaranteed within 5 turns it could be far more than that you see the odds don't state the time period number of players or any number of other variables used to calculate the win ratio so there might be times when it pays out frequently and other times where it doesn't it's also worth noting that unlike slot machines loot box win ratios aren't by law because for the most part they aren't considered gambling just a side note if you're watching this from the future and they are now then congratulations we did it but right now the rules are completely set by the house gamblers fallacy is used to manipulate the play into believing that the more unlucky turns they have the closer it brings them to a win apex legends even has what they call bad luck protection the guarantees that you'll receive at least one legendary item every thirty apex packs how generous of them this further reinforces the fallacy that if you play long enough a win is inevitable.

**Для Напарника:** Не показывать «ещё одна попытка, и будет редкость»; находки не должны имитировать лутбоксы.



# 🎬 Designing AI Allies - How Games Create Great Party Members and Companions ~ Design Doc — Design Doc


### Компаньон должен вносить вклад, не забирая игру у игрока  ·  [00:48](https://youtu.be/tIze-mre0ss?t=48)
**Мысль:** Слишком полезный союзник играет вместо игрока, слишком слабый становится обузой или забывается.

> So you need to rely on AI to control your allies. Designing and fine tuning their automated behavior can make or break an entire game. If they’re too useful or powerful you risk them playing the game for you. If they’re too weak or ineffective they could become an unintentional liability or you might forget that they're even there. In a perfect world these characters should feel like they make a real contribution without stealing the fun from the player.

**Для Напарника:** Кот-напарник присутствует и поддерживает, но не делает задачу за человека.


### Палико: компаньон как источник бонусов, а не опоры; автономия без обузы  ·  [01:22](https://youtu.be/tIze-mre0ss?t=82)
**Мысль:** Позиционирование союзника как дающего бонусы вместо ключевого помощника меняет ожидания; нет серьёзного штрафа за потерю здоровья; и это милый кот.

> There's an idea in psychology called Loss Aversion that says that losing something feels more negative than gaining it feels positive. One way to design an AI around Loss Aversion is instead of depending on a character and being disappointed if they fail to help, switch it up and have an AI character occasionally give out bonuses. Just framing the player’s expectations differently can make them happier, even if they’re getting the same benefits on average either way. The palicos of Monster Hunter World are a good example of how to design around Loss Aversion. Palicos are great at distracting monsters and creating opportunities during fights. They don’t do much damage, but they make up for it in utility and customization. They can be equipped with status-inflicting weapons and special palico tools that can heal you, give you buffs, set up traps, or they can even steal monster materials. They will use these skills on their own but you can command them manually if you want to. There’s no major penalty if they run out of health, either. They have autonomy without being a liability. Some palico equipment loadouts are better than others, but they all provide at least SOME benefit, but never so much that they make the monster hunting at the core of the game too easy. If Monster Hunter framed palicos as allies that did a major part of the monster hunting, the same behaviors would feel disappointing. But since they're more of a companion that gives out bonuses, and they only show up when you don’t have many actual players in your party, they feel like good partners. They're great during the beginning of the game while you figure out the ropes, and they don't get in the way of high-end monster hunting. And above all, it’s a cat. It’s adorable. Don’t overthink it.

**Для Напарника:** Кот как приятный бонус (находки, реплики) без вины, если он «не помог»; смерть и штрафы исключены.


### Расхождение между заявленной ролью союзника и тем, что он делает  ·  [05:12](https://youtu.be/tIze-mre0ss?t=312)
**Мысль:** Если игра говорит «команда», а делает «шоу одного героя», создаётся разочарование.

> Ally design can also become a problem if you don't properly frame what the AI members should be responsible for. In Star Fox Assault, there's a disconnect between what the game SAYS the AI characters are there for and what the game actually DOES with them.

**Для Напарника:** Роль кота (присутствие, а не надзор) должна совпадать с его поведением на всех экранах.


### Микроменеджмент приемлем, когда он заявлен как часть игры (gambits в FFXII)  ·  [07:11](https://youtu.be/tIze-mre0ss?t=431)
**Мысль:** Одна и та же ручная настройка раздражает в одной игре и радует в другой: решает ожидание игрока.

> Just make the AI yourself, you should try Final Fantasy XII. The gambit system in FFXII lets you program exactly the steps, or ‘gambits’, that your AI party members will take in battle in a kind of Lego brick sort of way. For example, you can set a gambit to have a character cast healing spells whenever someone’s HP drops below a set threshold, or automatically cast Haste on the party whenever you can. More advanced and specific conditions for gambits can be found in shops as the game progresses. You also have to consider the priorities of your gambits, placing more situational instructions at the top and common ones at the bottom. If you have attacking nearby enemies as a top priority, the other gambits will never be used. It's not that far off in concept from what an actual AI programmer might code, but this way you feel like you’re in control if the AI screws something up. Even though the gambit system needs micromanaging like in Kingdom Hearts the framing is completely different. Managing gambits is a huge part of the game, so it's not as frustrating as the micromanaging was in Kingdom Hearts. Players expect it, so they’re OK with putting in the time.

**Для Напарника:** Настройки Напарника скрыты по умолчанию; кому нужно, найдёт, и не будет раздражаться.


### Атрей: всегда ощущается как вклад, одна кнопка, не нужно микроменеджмента  ·  [08:33](https://youtu.be/tIze-mre0ss?t=513)
**Мысль:** Помощник стреляет, оглушает, подсказывает и решает головоломки, не превращаясь в эскорт; нарушение послушности подаётся как осмысленный сюжетный ход.

> God of War 4’s story is all about the father-son relationship between Kratos and Atreus. The developers at Santa Monica designed Atreus to always feel like he was contributing during combat. He can shoot arrows and summon creatures on your command with a single button. He’ll follow up on your attacks when he’s nearby and he can distract and stun enemies and open them up for you. You don’t have to micromanage him beyond equipping stat boosting gear and occasionally saving him when he gets caught by an enemy. Atreus also acts as your hint guide and helps you solve puzzles. He’s not treated like an escort character but the game does turn him into a liability in combat to fit with some of the story beats. In a later section of the game, Atreus won’t always listen to your commands and will recklessly lead you into bad situations. He’ll even start using the special summon attacks on his own regardless if it's well timed or not. For some this sequence can come off as rushed, but it's a deliberate choice and it's an effective way to tie together an antagonistic AI character in the story with their behavior in the gameplay.

**Для Напарника:** Кот реагирует на события по одному касанию; необычное поведение допустимо только как заслуженный сюжетный момент.


### Неконтролируемые раздражающие действия союзника оправданы только сюжетной причиной  ·  [10:21](https://youtu.be/tIze-mre0ss?t=621)
**Мысль:** Без причины ничто не уравновешивает раздражение от плохих действий, которыми нельзя управлять.

> With a good story driven reason, even the most annoying character actions can have purpose and make the game better. But without a reason there's nothing left to counter the raw annoyance that uncontrollable bad actions inherently have.

**Для Напарника:** Кот не должен делать то, что нельзя отключить или управлять (звук, реплики).


### Баланс: помощь без захвата игры и ввод без постоянного ведения за руку  ·  [11:48](https://youtu.be/tIze-mre0ss?t=708)
**Мысль:** Ожидания игрока и корректная рамка превращают ИИ в союзника.

> AI design is hard to do. Developers have to find the balance between an AI that feels like a help without taking over the game, between an AI that takes input from players but still doesn’t need their hand held all the time. Lots of games get the balance wrong for lots of reasons. But by learning from mistakes made in the past, taking some cues from psychology, and setting expectations properly you can turn an AI into an ally.

**Для Напарника:** Чат с котом: просить минимум ввода, давать значимую помощь, не вести за руку.

# 🎬 Exploring Emotional Attachment to Game Characters — ACM SIGCHI


### Привязанность бывает разной окраски: сила, восхищение, забота  ·  [07:22](https://youtu.be/yO17SkxIPeA?t=442)

> That the relatedness to game characters comes with different flavors so while all participants reported a sense of relatedness the emotional quality because the emotional quality varied considerably so for instance of feeling empowered when diagnosed by Anita or feeling worried for Ellie and caring for her well-being as such these insights enable a more nuanced evaluation of character attachment and may also inform game design to envision a specific emotional character experiences.

**Для Напарника:** Выбрать целевую эмоцию связи с котом (спокойное присутствие и тёплая забота), а не смешивать несколько.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус как маленькая задача: цель, инструмент (таймер, шаг) и ощущение, что научился делать.


### Новичок без ментальной модели путается, если игра не говорит, что верно  ·  [03:15](https://youtu.be/XA1F8r1D8eg?t=195)
**Мысль:** Интуиция строится на прежнем опыте, а человек без игрового опыта дольше начинает и иначе трактует взаимодействия.

> Intuition for games is because we come in with an existing mental model we've played similar games we've interacted with similar things in the real world so we have a set of expectations and we're able to make predictions about how these things will work in this new game in rasputin's video what games are like for someone who doesn't play games we see what actually happens when somebody who doesn't have that mental database built up tries to learn about the game and it takes longer to get started interacting with things in the way that the designer intended and they interpret interactions differently than any veteran player would if a player hasn't played similar games it's much easier for them to get confused when the game doesn't adequately tell them what's right and wrong remember this is our world and we're asking them to learn the rules of it.

**Для Напарника:** Онбординг Напарника не должен предполагать игровую грамотность: явные подсказки, что нажать и что произойдёт.


### Без обратной связи нет обучения: ответы без оценки бесполезны  ·  [04:10](https://youtu.be/XA1F8r1D8eg?t=250)
**Мысль:** Тесты без проверки не дают знать, правильно ли ты отвечал; обратная связь нужна, чтобы обновлять ментальную модель.

> Now imagine taking a math class in university and every week you're taught new material each element building up one after another each week you take a quiz and answer a bunch of questions but that quiz is never graded you don't know which answers are right or wrong at the end of the year after taking tons of quizzes without getting any feedback how well do you think you'll score on a test this is a crucial step in the analysis part of the scientific method feedback we perform a test so that we get a result so that we can know if we were right or wrong and we use that feedback to inform future experiments in games like in science if we want a better mental model we need to get good feedback.

**Для Напарника:** Каждое действие в приложении должно получать понятный отклик (в голосе напарника): сработало или нет.


### Игровой цикл: ментальная модель, цель, действие, правила, обратная связь, обновление  ·  [05:08](https://youtu.be/XA1F8r1D8eg?t=308)
**Мысль:** Игрок исходит из модели, ставит цель, выбирает действие, правила мира реагируют последовательно, обратная связь обновляет модель. Последовательность правил укрепляет уверенность в обучаемости мира.

> Okay so what is the game loop then the game loop much like the loop involved with the scientific method is all about solving problems it's about learning skills it's about being presented with questions and doing your best with the tools provided to answer them sufficiently the idea of the game loop is very well described by spryfox co-founder dan cook's 2012 essay loops and arcs available on his lost guarded blog and also in his igda seattle talk game design theory i wish i knew we start with a mental model and what do we know about this game and things related to this game what goals do we have in mind in terms of the scientific method what's our question and what's our hypothesis maybe we're fighting a boss in a zelda game with a big yellow eye and based on our experience with previous zelda games we can predict that hitting the boss in the big yellow eye will deal the most damage maybe we're playing a puzzle game with ice cubes on an icy floor and we know from real life that ice is slippery and we might be able to push them around once we have a goal we instinctively predict which actions might be available to us and choose an action which we think is going to bring us closer to that goal no way nope okay victoria because this is our own world the next step is to have the rules of the game take place it's important for us as designers to consider this step because we're the ones designing the cause and effect of actions we want our actions to behave consistently to reinforce a strong mental model and boost the player's confidence that our game world really is learnable finally the all crucial feedback we give the player some kind of data that they can analyze and use to update their mental model this could be in the form of rewards like coins points or access to new areas or it could be in the form of evocative stimuli like animations or sound effects but of course this is a loop once we have our feedback this updates our mental model we have a better understanding of the game's rules and we can either try again at our goal or we can formulate a new goal and take actions towards that.

**Для Напарника:** Использовать цикл как чек-лист для Фокуса: цель ясна -> действие -> согласованная реакция кота/острова -> награда, обновляющая ожидания.


### Линза петли: сигналы, обратная связь и количество вопросов к игроку  ·  [10:20](https://youtu.be/XA1F8r1D8eg?t=620)
**Мысль:** Надо проверять, видны ли цели и действия, даёт ли петля обратную связь и не слишком ли много вопросов одновременно; плейтесты как учебные упражнения показывают, где петля ломается.

> Well the loop asks us to look at what problems the player is solving how often and how exactly we're going about reinforcing them a key insight with the game loop is the importance of looking out for signs and feedback does our loop adequately signal available goals and actions and does it provide adequate feedback to teach the player more about the game rules after they take an action once we know the problems that are being solved and we've identified our loops we can start considering the player's questions while they engage in those loops how much health do i have left how does that enemy die which enemies should i hit first is the player asking interesting questions are we providing tools to answer those questions are we asking too many questions at once looking at the game as loops as cycles of learning and problem solving we can evaluate play tests as learning exercises and sort out where the loop is breaking for players.

**Для Напарника:** Проверять каждый экран: не задаём ли сразу много вопросов (принцип одного вопроса за визит).



# 🎬 Gameplay Loops Are Out, Chains Are In — Indie Game Clinic


### Цепочка действий нарастает эмоционально  ·  [02:56](https://youtu.be/6SYX17NzNqE?t=176)
**Мысль:** В комбо каждое успешное действие повышает ставки следующего; ошибка на четвёртом-пятом шаге ощущается сильнее, чем на первом. У цепочки есть эмоциональная дуга и нарастающая вовлечённость.

> I think chains in games are interesting because of how they map on to emotion. And the most simple chain that I can think of that most people would probably think of if you said a chain of actions in a game would be like executing a combo in a fighting game. >> When you commit to doing a specific set or string of actions in a particular order, what happens is every one that you successfully complete raises the stakes about what's coming next. If you're trying to complete five actions in a row and you get the first or second one wrong, you're going to feel a bit bummed out. Oh no. When you're getting wrong on the fourth or fifth one, that is when it starts to feel like an epic fail. Oh, I almost had it. There is a emotional arc to a chain. You're not button bashing. You're trying to hit a very specific list of things in a specific order. It creates this rising sense of emotional investment with each thing you do.

**Для Напарника:** Очередь шагов (Zeigarnik) использует эту механику; осторожно: обрыв поздней цепочки больнее, поэтому нужны мягкие реплики кота при срыве.


### Ценность цепочек: сбор ресурса значим из-за цели  ·  [04:03](https://youtu.be/6SYX17NzNqE?t=243)
**Мысль:** Подбор палок сам по себе не весел; смысл ему даёт то, что игрок планирует с ними сделать и как это служит эмоциональной фантазии игры (Dan Cook).

> There is an essay by game designer Dan Cook. I will link it in the description. You should go and read it. It is about value chains. If you've got a economic chain in a game, like you collect these things and you use them for this. It's the what you use them for that gives the collection meaning and makes you feel something about it. So the example that Dan gives is picking up sticks is not inherently fun. You can make it as fun as you can. You can make it feel really toyike and they could be really sparkly sticks or whatever, but it's not an inherently fun activity. It's not enough to carry a game. What makes the activity enjoyable is what the player plans to do with the sticks and how that fulfills the like core fantasy of the game, the core emotional thing that the game is pulling at.

**Для Напарника:** Сессия Фокуса значима, когда видна цель: монетки или энергия идут на конкретное развитие острова.


### Конечная цель делает смысловым каждое действие в пути  ·  [04:55](https://youtu.be/6SYX17NzNqE?t=295)
**Мысль:** В Fallout 4 строительство превратило хлам в осмысленные ресурсы, и решения «что поднять» стали богаче. Понимание цели делает сбор интереснее.

> Another really good example of this would be there was a little bit of crafting in Fallout 3 and then in Fallout New Vegas there was a little bit more crafting and then in Fallout 4 you're now like the mayor of Boston and you need to rebuild Boston using twigs and super glue. There are people that don't like those base building mechanics. quite possible to play the game while ignoring those mechanics. But my experience of it was personally being able to build stuff out of the junk in the game changed everything. It meant that objects that previously were just a piece of junk to be collected and sold and maybe you would think a little bit about like the cost to weight ratio of different objects as you picked them up, they were now imbued with a different sort of meaning because now I'm trying to build the lighting for my 1950s Hawaiian Aloha bar and I need to look out for items that have copper wiring in them. So, I'm learning which fans and telephones have copper wiring in them. the destination of the chain that we know we're working towards changes the feel of engaging with mechanics earlier on because knowing that I'm trying to rebuild Boston with twigs and glue means that every time I go into a place and I'm looting it, the decision space of what do I pick up, what do I leave behind is suddenly richer because I'm not just taking every gun to sell at the merchant because that has the best weight to cost ratio or something like that. having a kind of forward destination for these resources I'm collecting. The action of collecting the stuff becomes more meaningful and interesting and exciting to do because the player understands what they are trying to achieve by doing it.

**Для Напарника:** Показывать, к какой постройке ведёт текущий шаг или сессия, чтобы малые действия получали смысл.


### Скрытые цепочки секретов: игрок чувствует себя умным  ·  [06:36](https://youtu.be/6SYX17NzNqE?t=396)
**Мысль:** Секретные цепочки (фейри, скрытая концовка) дают открытие, когда человек проходит мимо десятки раз и затем собирает картину. Особенно работают в играх с повторными забегами.

> Spelunky has a chain in it of interesting secrets that lead up to kind of the proper ending of the game. It's not the only game that's done this. Castlevania Symphony of the Night had a similar thing. Basically, in both games, you can do what you think is completing the game. And then there's a hidden better way of completing the game. In order to do it, you'd have to stumble across this chain of secrets or you'd have to read about it online or something like that. I think that's cool. That's a chain where the player doesn't really know it's a chain. And I've been trying to incorporate some of these into the game that I'm currently working on. There are these fairies. They're reasonably trivial to spawn, but the first few times the player manages to spawn these fairies, they're not actually going to know what triggered it unless they read it on the internet. They won't really know how they did it. And the fairies will be part of a chain where if the player, and by doing that, they'll have unlocked that and then they'll feel really good about themselves. But they'll probably encounter the fairies quite a few times before they realize that they're part of a chain. and that there is a very simple way to start that chain and to spawn the fairies. They just they'll be doing it by accident to start off with. I have a similar plan for this horrific mouth monster. This is going to be the start of a chain. And if you feed it a I think this type of design is interesting in a systemic game like a rogike because the player is doing lots and lots of runs of doing the same thing and that vastly increases their chance of just stumbling across the solution to one of these things. It's kind of how the chains work in Spalunky. It's how some of the puzzle chains work in blueprints. I really, really like that feeling of you walk past something dozens of times before you realize that it's significant and it's been under your nose all this time and then you put the pieces together and there's something really pleasing about that. And I love the depth that can add to a game which has relatively simple controls.

**Для Напарника:** Спрятать на острове редкие находки, зависящие от регулярного присутствия, и открывать их без подсказки, как «заслуженное» дофаминовое открытие.


### Два вида цепочек: явная цель и скрытое открытие  ·  [09:09](https://youtu.be/6SYX17NzNqE?t=549)
**Мысль:** Явные цепочки определяются мотивацией построить нечто; скрытые дают чувство «ага, я сам догадался».

> You can get clear chains where players know where they're going and the feeling of the chain is defined by the player's motivation to build a particular thing. Or you can get these weird secret chains where the player feels smart because aha now I realized that I was actually making the fairies spawn by.

**Для Напарника:** Сочетать явные цели (следующий ориентир) и тихие скрытые находки на острове.



# 🎬 Gamification to improve our world: Yu-kai Chou at TEDxLausanne — TEDx Talks


### Геймификация — не очки/бейджи/лидерборды, а мотивация через базовые драйвы  ·  [01:38](https://youtu.be/v5Qjuegtiyc?t=98)
**Мысль:** Элементы игры сами по себе не делают игру весёлой (большинство игр скучны); сначала нужно понять, какие драйвы мотивируют человека.

> So what is gamification? Gamification is the craft to take all those fun and exciting elements of games and pouring them into boring non-game context. Things you have to do, but you don't necessarily want to do. So when I say gamification, some of you might start thinking about what we call the PBLs, the points, badges, and leaderboards. And this is where a lot of people think, "Oh, if I just take points and put into a product or badges, it'll make it fun and exciting." And that's a big misconception. When I started in gamification in 2003, it was a very lonely passion for me. Very few people cared about it and very few people understood it. Now about 3-4 years ago, gamification became a buzzword and a lot more people and companies, organizations, schools started to care about it. But still very few people truly understand it. So if you think about what makes a game fun, if you ask gamers that, they're not going to say, "Oh, because the game has points." They'll say, "Oh, because it challenges me, it makes me use my creativity, it allows me to hang with friends, it makes me be more than who I am today." And if you think about it, every single game out there has game elements and game mechanics in them. But most games are still boring. Most games are still not engaging. So it's very naive to think that, "Oh, if I just take these game elements and mechanics from that you can find in games that are even boring and pour them into products and experiences, it'll automatically make it super fun and exciting." So what we know here is that good gamification does not start with game elements, but really starts with how it motivates our core drives.

**Для Напарника:** Остров — это не набор очков, а ощущение роста и принадлежности; избегать PBL-шаблона.


### DragonBox: скучная математика замаскирована под кормление дракона, ребёнок «не знает», что учится  ·  [08:08](https://youtu.be/v5Qjuegtiyc?t=488)
**Мысль:** Правила вводятся ступенями, сложность растёт, обратная связь вкус/«фу» и одна-три звезды мотивируют улучшать решение; цель игры — питомец.

> DragonBox is a learning game that gets little kids between 5 to 9-year-olds to be obsessed with solving hundreds of middle school algebra questions. Now, I don't know about you, but when I was a kid, math was the ultimate school grind. Like everything else could be fun, you know, physics can be fun, science, history can be fun, but math, no. Math is not fun. Math you just sit there and you have to do it so your parents don't get mad at you. So check out how DragonBox gets this to be fun for little kids. So the premise of the game is that you have a box and inside the box there's a baby dragon. This baby dragon only wants to come out when nothing's around it so it can eat. So this is the onboarding stage, teaching the rules. These green circles you see are zeros in disguise. Zero means nothing, so you tap on it, disappears. Zero means nothing. It scans, the dragon wants to come out and eat happy. Now on stage three, it introduces some basic math principles. Positive robot and negative robot cancel each other out, become zero. Negative two and positive two cancel each other out, become zero. And now the dragon scans and the dragon wants to come out eat again. And you can see the little dragon turns from a egg to a little baby. Nice. At one point it gets pretty complex. The DragonBox becomes a X and you're trying to isolate that X. So you have see you have to do things like balance both sides of the equation, multiply everything by two. You also need to make sure the numbers are optimally solved. So no two over four. So you can see in this process, the child is trying to make sure something this is optimally solved and the dragon will eat it because it's yummy. But the other two, they're not, so dragon refuses to eat it. That those will be yuck. As you can see, it'll be yum, yuck, yuck. You know, bad numbers. Dragons don't like bad numbers. So the child only gets one star out of three. So now the child doesn't even know he's doing math. He's just trying to feed his dragon. Grow his dragon. He's like, "Oh, why don't I get three stars? I got to figure out a better way to isolate this so the dragon wants to eat everything.".

**Для Напарника:** Питомец/кот как смысл действия: микрошаги становятся «кормлением» острова; обучение правилам через игру, а не инструкции.


### Scarcity: Kickstarter — приманка, таймер, последняя миля, групповая миссия  ·  [11:01](https://youtu.be/v5Qjuegtiyc?t=661)
**Мысль:** Дефицит и срочность сильны, потому что запретное желаннее; Kickstarter сочетает приз, обратный отсчёт, «осталось немного» и общий квест.

> Want something just because you can't have it. Like if grapes are on the table, you really don't care about the grapes. But if they're on a shelf just beyond your reach, you're always thinking about those grapes, you know, can I have them? When can I have them? Are they even sweet? And Kickstarter is a great example that utilizes a lot of great game design techniques. They first dangle this great reward, this great prize in front of you, cool technology. Then they show you what we call a countdown timer, 21 days to go, you have to act now. If you don't act now, you won't get this prize. So, the sense of urgency. Then it shows you what we call the last mile drive. Oh, there's only $3,000 left, only 2,000 more and we'll get it, you'll get it. And finally, to all together, this what we call a group quest. You can only get You can only accomplish the quest when a entire team will move forward together on it.

**Для Напарника:** Осторожно: срочность и дефицит — чёрная шляпа; использовать только «последнюю милю» (ещё один шаг до рассвета на острове) без давления.


### Speed cam lottery: награда за хорошее поведение через лотерею снизила скорость на 20%  ·  [12:06](https://youtu.be/v5Qjuegtiyc?t=726)
**Мысль:** Неопределённая награда за соблюдение правила мотивирует сильнее штрафа за нарушение.

> This is speed cam lottery. How it works is whenever you drive by the speed cam lottery and you're speeding, it will take a picture of your car and it'll give you a fine, pretty straightforward. But when you're going by the speed cam lottery and you're within the speed limit, it'll enter you into a lottery pot where you can you when you have a chance to win the money from those who are fined. So, even though it your chance of winning is fairly low and it's not a lot a lot of money to begin with, because everyone thinks that, "Oh, maybe I'll win this time. Maybe I'll win this time." You try You change your behavior, slow down, huh, you know? So, speed cam lottery successfully reduces the speed of passing cars by 20%.

**Для Напарника:** Редкие приятные сюрпризы после завершённой сессии (редкая находка) — награда за выполненное, а не наказание за невыполненное.



# 🎬 Here's What Makes Incremental Games FUN! — Developing Ideal Idle Incremental Game


### Качели доходов и затрат: линейный рост против экспоненциальной цены  ·  [03:08](https://youtu.be/hty_r4LIRb0?t=188)
**Мысль:** Каждый генератор даёт линейный прирост, а стоит на 10% дороже предыдущего, поэтому ожидание растёт от секунд до недель и игрок упирается в стену.

> The vast majority of idle incremental games are just a seesaw between profits and costs this means that with every next generator you buy your profits grow linearly while your costs grow exponentially to make this clearer let's take a look at the formulas I'm using for a do game average generator you buy will increase your dock production by two ducks per second this means that 10 generators will produce 20 ducks per second and a 100 generators will produce 200 docks per second that's what linear growth is however the cost of each subsequent generator will be 10% higher than previous one given this exponential growth the cost of the 10 generator will be 24 docks and the 100s one will cost more than 100,000 ducks that escalated quickly now what do these numbers really mean let's say you already have nine generators producing 18 docks per second it will only take you two seconds to purchase your 10 generator but you would need to wait more than 10 minutes to progress from 99 to 100 generators and if you've already purchased 199 generators it would take more than 50 days to buy the 200 one which would cost more than two billion docks That's The Power of exponential growth so at the earlier stages of the game your production will exceed costs but the more generators you buy the higher the price of the next one becomes until eventually the waiting time for the next generators becomes prohibitive and you hit the wall.

**Для Напарника:** Для кривой роста острова избегать стен, из-за которых человек чувствует «застрял»; кривая должна оставаться посильной.


### Веселье — в изобретательных способах пробить стену  ·  [04:50](https://youtu.be/hty_r4LIRb0?t=290)
**Мысль:** Вся вовлечённость инкрементальной игры рождается из разных способов преодоления стены; автор считает двух базовых способов достаточно для хорошей игры.

> Remember the one and only mechanic that makes incremental games fun I mentioned about well in my opinion all the enjoyment and engagement of idle inal game stems from its ability to offer creative ways to overcome this wall potentially there are tens or even hundreds approaches to it covering them all would definitely be beyond the scope of this video however there are two basic and essential ones that without exaggeration are used in every incremental game I ever played and I firmly believe that using just these two are sufficient to create a reasonably engaging game.

**Для Напарника:** Если рост замедляется, давать человеку интересный выбор (новое существо, новый способ), а не просто больше ожидания.


### Новые типы генераторов: дорогие, но выгоднее из-за экспоненты  ·  [05:36](https://youtu.be/hty_r4LIRb0?t=336)
**Мысль:** Второй тип генератора стоит дороже, но из-за экспоненциальной цены первого оказывается выгоднее; число типов выбирает дизайнер.

> So once we hit the wall and the speed of our game slows down what can be done to speed it up the most obvious and widely used solution is to create another type of generator whose price would be much higher but whose production is also greater in our case we create a second type of generator which will cost one 1,000 ducks and will produce 100 Ducks per second at first glance it might seem that this generator is less preferable because its production rate compared to its price is lower however due to the exponential nature of price growth in fact this second type of generator produces more ducks and costs less let me show you an example you will spend more than 11,000 ducks to buy 50 generators of the first type which produce 100 Ducks per second this same production will cost you only 1,000 Ducks if you buy the generator of the second type now it will cost you almost 1 and a half million ducks to purchase 100 generators of the first type producing 200 Ducks per second but you can reach that same level of production by buying just two generators of the second type for 2,100 Ducks I hope that makes sense at the same time doesn't really matter how much more appealing the second type of generator is because sooner or later it will also become irrelevant only the game designer decides how many types of generators there will be in the game for the Duck game I limited myself for three types of generators the last one produces 5,000 ducks per second and it cost starts from 100,000 ducks in my main game project get a little gold there are 15 types of generators ranging from fan to a white hole.

**Для Напарника:** Вводить для острова новые типы построек с разной ролью и выгодой, а не только больше одного и того же.


### Престиж: сброс прогресса в обмен на постоянный бонус  ·  [07:46](https://youtu.be/hty_r4LIRb0?t=466)
**Мысль:** Престиж-механика позволяет сбросить прогресс ради постоянного бонуса и решить, ждать ли ради большего бонуса.

> Well the second approach to Breaking the wall is the well-known Prestige mechanic again if you play it incremental games before you almost suddenly come across the concept of prestige Mechanic In A not it's a mechanic that allows you to reset the progress in the game in exchange for some permanent boost that allows you to progress faster next time for our Duck game Let's implement the most basic Prestige mechanic first we should add the variable to keep track of all ducks produced during the game now once the number of ducks reaches 1 million will'll show a button offering to reset the progress and obtain a 100% boost to all duck production we can either Prestige now or wait longer to collect more ducks and increase our potential bonus each subsequent step require 1 million more Ducks than previous one and we'll add another 100% to the bonus so to reach 200% bonus we need to collect a total of 3 million docks and to reach 300% bonus we need to collect 6 million Ducks.

**Для Напарника:** Не применимо: сброс прогресса противоречит принципу «ничего не отнимать»; допустима только добровольная смена острова (сезон) без потери накопленного.



# 🎬 Hooked by Nir Eyal | Full Audiobook 🎧 — WE WE TV


### Переменная награда: тайна и её разрешение создают тягу  ·  [04:13](https://youtu.be/TW_yZhdWt0g?t=253)
**Мысль:** Мозг получает удовольствие от разрешения неопределённости; предсказуемая обратная связь тягу не создаёт.

> Which leads us directly into phase three, variable reward. This is where we create a craving. A highly predictable feedback loop, like say opening your fridge and the light turning on, doesn't create a craving. You expect it. But what if a totally different delicious treat magically appeared every single time you open that door? Now you're intrigued. To understand this, think about a baby meeting a dog for the very first time. The baby's initially filled with uncertainty. What is this hairy thing? Is it going to hurt me? But as that mystery resolves and they realize the dog is friendly, that tension releases into an absolute explosion of giggles. Our brains are literally wired to find the resolution of uncertainty deeply satisfying. But what happens a few years later? The child knows exactly how the dog will react. The mystery's gone and the intense novelty completely wears off. The cycle of conflict, mystery, and resolution is as old as storytelling itself. And it's exactly what keeps us hooked. Think about hit TV shows like Breaking Bad. They leverage this exact same variability. Why do we binge watch? Because the writers introduce a massive conflict, they create this agonizing mystery, and right when they finally resolve it, boom, they introduce a brand new problem. The viewer is left in total suspense, chemically craving the resolution that they can only get by letting the next episode play. It's a variable reward system perfectly tuned to human psychology.

**Для Напарника:** Реveal на финале сессии строить как «тайна → разрешение» (что вырастет на острове), а не как плоский результат.


### Конечная и бесконечная вариативность: FarmVille против Twitter  ·  [05:28](https://youtu.be/TW_yZhdWt0g?t=328)
**Мысль:** Когда механика разгадана, игра с конечной вариативностью умирает; контент от пользователей остаётся непредсказуемым долго.

> But here's the catch. Not all variability is created equal. We have finite variability and infinite variability. Remember the game FarmVille? It exploded in popularity, but it relied on finite variability. Eventually, users completely figured out the mechanics, the game became predictable, and millions of people just abandoned it. On the other hand, platforms like Twitter or Pinterest use infinite variability. Because the content is constantly being generated by millions of other users, the stream is always changing. It never gets completely predictable, and that is what sustains user interest for years on end.

**Для Напарника:** Остров должен иметь неисчерпаемое разнообразие находок (много ориентиров, времена суток, редкие события), иначе привыкание.



# 🎬 Hooked by Nir Eyal: The Full Summary — Yapping Books


### Назвать, какую награду даёшь: племя, охота или самость  ·  [04:50](https://youtu.be/0A-Do6ePZyY?t=290)
**Мысль:** Знание типа награды проясняет, какую механику копировать; многие плохие заимствования берут механику продукта, который тянет за другую струну.

> Now, the third phase, the one the book is best known for. Imagine a button that gives you exactly the same thing every single time you press it. Interesting once, dull by the hundredth press. Now, imagine you do not know what is coming. Sometimes ordinary, sometimes remarkable. That is the variable reward. The user still gets what they came for, >> >> but not in the same shape every time. It is why scrolling feels so different from reading a page. Most posts are forgettable. One is funny. One message matters. >> >> One video is exactly right. You cannot tell which is coming, and that uncertainty is what keeps the search alive. And he groups them three ways. Rewards of the tribe, approval, belonging, attention from other people. Rewards of the hunt, the search for information or resources, and rewards of the self, mastery, competence, finishing something. Likes are the tribe. A feed you troll for something good is the hunt. Clearing an inbox or leveling up is the self. What I found useful was not the categories for their own sake. It is that naming which reward you actually offer is clarifying. Because most bad feature copying I have seen comes from lifting a mechanic off a product that was pulling a completely different string.

**Для Напарника:** Напарник даёт награду «самости» (завершил, вырос остров), а не «племени» (лайки).



# 🎬 How a handful of tech companies control billions of minds every day | Tristan Harris — TED


### Пример: друг отменил ужин — приложение могло бы вести к людям, а не к ленте  ·  [08:43](https://youtu.be/C74amJRp730?t=523)
**Мысль:** В момент одиночества дизайнеры хотят максимизировать время на экране, хотя могли бы помочь выйти к близким.
