**Для Напарника:** Мерка для Напарника: не требует гиперфокуса и рефлексов, ощущение комфорта важнее набора фич.



# 🎬 The Tamagotchi Was A Huge Success. But Why? | Mental Floss — Mental Floss


### Существо становится милым только когда о нём заботятся  ·  [05:34](https://youtu.be/PTemp5C4qAM?t=334)
**Мысль:** Тамагочи растёт из безликого комка в «кавай» существо лишь при заботе; вложенные усилия превращают милоту в привязанность.

> Maita had conducted extensive market research on their target demographic of girls in junior high school who loved the name tamagotchi and the design of the creature itself which started life as an ill-defined blob of tissue that came from a planet tamagotchi and slowly grew into something prized by teenage girls in japan what's known as kawaii or cute but it only became adorable if it was played with and tended to with care if not it might turn into a virtual delinquent poorly behaved or worse dead.

**Для Напарника:** Остров/кот могут «расцветать» от действий пользователя (рост через вложенные сессии), а при пропуске просто останавливаться, не деградировать.



# 🎬 This Psychological Trick Makes Rewards Backfire — Game Maker's Toolkit


### Квесты в Don't Starve научили играть, но убили свободную игру  ·  [01:19](https://youtu.be/1ypOUn6rThM?t=79)
**Мысль:** Игроки оптимизировали ради квеста, избегали риска и потеряли мотивацию, когда квесты кончились; решением стали тонкие подсказки в UI.

> When Klei was making the initial prototype for its sandbox survival game Don't Starve, they quickly realised that testers had no idea how to play the game - and they instantly became stuck. So the testers were given a few hints - and once they got over the hump, they were able to experiment, explore, and started to have a lot of fun. In response, Klei decided to create a series of small, tutorial-like quests to help players get started. Survive this many nights. Find this many items. That sort of thing. And it worked! But only so much as players learned how to play the game. Because beyond that, the quests were a complete and utter disaster. Klei discovered that players focused exclusively on those quests, and thought of everything else as a noisy distraction. They optimised their play in really boring ways in order to finish the quest at hand. They avoided doing anything risky, because it meant they might fail. And then they became completely demotivated the second the quests ran out. Klei says "In structuring the game as a series of explicit tasks to be completed, we taught the player to depend upon those tasks to create meaning in the game". In the end, Klei solved its onboarding problem by tweaking the UI to give players subtle hints about how to get started - such as highlighting the most important items you can craft. But the quests were left on the cutting room floor - leaving players to learn for themselves. Because if a game is about experimentation, exploration, or player-guided discovery - explicit goals can limit a player's creativity and imagination.

**Для Напарника:** Онбординг Напарника: тонкие подсказки вместо цепочки заданий «сделай X, получи Y», чтобы не подменить внутренний мотив.


### Цель, которую ставишь сам, сильнее заданной; рекорды бесконечны, чек-листы конечны  ·  [03:11](https://youtu.be/1ypOUn6rThM?t=191)
**Мысль:** Личные рекорды и сравнение с распределением других мотивируют сильнее порога достижения; чек-лист можно закончить и остановиться.

> Okay, here's another story. Zach Barth makes problem-solving puzzle games about designing your own automated machines, like Exapunks and Shenzen I/O. In these games, you can make the machines however you like - if it works, it works. But it's actually really fun to go back in, and see if you can refine your creation to make it, say, smaller or faster. So in Zach's first two commercial games, Spacechem and Infinifactory, he added a few Steam achievements that encourage this sort of optimisation - like the Spacechem achievement "Beat the assignment "No Thanks Necessary" in under 2200 cycles." But, in all the games released after that - those achievements are completely gone. What's up with that? ZACH: "We wanted to add achievements because that was back when achievements were cool. That was back before I thought achievements were awful." ZACH: "The thing i don't like about them is that the game already has a reward system. We have something that's far more meaningful and far less arbitrary than a random threshold." What Zach's talking about is a bounty of metrics that you can use to gauge how well you've done. There's your own personal score. There's leaderboards that compare you to your Steam friends. And there are these brilliant histograms that show you how your solution stacks up in comparison to every other player. All of these - the strive to beat your personal best, or the drive to do better than other players - are extremely strong motivators to do better. As Zach says: "a goal that you set yourself is way more powerful than a goal someone else sets for you". So if a game is about improving yourself, a personal or social goal can be a stronger motivator than a set threshold. My final story comes from the adorable track-laying puzzle game Mini Metro. The game's developers wanted to focus on personal growth and high scores. And so - according to UI designer Jamie Churchman - the team specifically tried to avoid these goal and reward meta structures as they can become a "means to an end". For example, the game does have unlockable cities - which is just to limit player choice at the beginning of the game. But Jamie acknowledges that some people will play each city until the threshold, unlock the next one, and when they've unlocked all the cities they feel like they've finished the game and can stop playing. We should remember that goals are a checklist that can be completed. And like with Don't Starve, some players will exclusively rely on the game to give them purpose and direction. But measurements of your skill - such as leaderboards and scoring systems, have no finish: you can continue to improve on your personal best forever - which partly explains why we can still play Tetris after three decades.

**Для Напарника:** Показывать личную динамику («ты сегодня на 2 минуты дольше»), а не пороги и достижения; без таблиц лидеров, чтобы не стыдить.


### Цели и достижения в «внутренне мотивированных» играх сужают творчество и требуют вечной подпитки  ·  [07:42](https://youtu.be/1ypOUn6rThM?t=462)
**Мысль:** Явные цели ограничивают, ставят порог и обязывают разработчика постоянно подкидывать новые награды.

> Because there are certainly games that lean more towards intrinsic motivation. Like games that focus on exploration, creativity, expression, and growth. There are games where you set your own goals and expect no rewards in return. And so when more extrinsically motivating systems - like explicit goals, progression meters, and achievements - are added to these games, our motivation can take a hit. We become blinkered to creative solutions. We're less motivated to improve ourselves. We put an arbitrary threshold on how much we attain. And developers now need to create a constant drip feed of new goals and rewards, or risk losing us entirely.

**Для Напарника:** Не заваливать Дом ачивками и квестами; поддерживать любопытство, а не чек-лист.


### Как применять цели аккуратно: крупные, сравнительные, необязательные или скрытые  ·  [09:07](https://youtu.be/1ypOUn6rThM?t=547)
**Мысль:** Большие цели без пошаговых инструкций, личные рекорды вместо порогов, необязательные или скрытые достижения.

> For example, with goals - it's better to use large, overarching goals that players can complete however they want, rather than restrictive step-by-step instructions. You can focus on comparative metrics, like leaderboards, histograms, and personal bests, rather than absolute thresholds. Make goals optional, like Hitman's challenges, or hidden, like Outer Wilds' achievements.

**Для Напарника:** Цели в Напарнике — необязательные и мягкие; редкие находки скрыты, а не в списке.


### Play of the Game, монеты-подсказки Odyssey и Korok в BotW — приятные сюрпризы за любопытство  ·  [10:09](https://youtu.be/1ypOUn6rThM?t=609)
**Мысль:** Нинтендо не говорит как играть ради мифической награды; награды лежат там, где нормально смотреть, и поощряют исследование.

> An example of this in games might be Overwatch's Play of the Game, which is a short highlight reel showcasing the best moment in the match. It doesn't really do anything, but it's a huge boost to the ego of the player who gets the starring role. And this is all over Nintendo's latest blockbusters. In Odyssey, there's nothing telling you to clamber up here with Mario's advanced move-set: but here's a cheeky cache of coins as a pat on the back. And in Breath of the Wild, every suspicious nook could be a reward, like a Korok seed. As Nintendo's Bill Trinen says: "When they create their games, don't tell you how to play their game in order to achieve some kind of mythical reward. There are things you can do in the game that will result in some sort of reward or unexpected surprise. In my mind, that really encourages the sense of exploration rather than the sense of 'If I do that, I'm going to get some sort of artificial point or score:.

**Для Напарника:** Тайники на острове и редкие реплики напарника за необычные действия, без заранее обещанных очков.



# 🎬 TIKTOK'S ADDICTIVE PSYCHOLOGY EXPLAINED IN UNDER 7 MINUTES!!! (BEHAVIORAL SCIENCE!) — Pete Judo


### Переменное вознаграждение: непредсказуемость делает свайп затягивающим  ·  [02:27](https://youtu.be/sxJ6XlUfauM?t=147)
**Мысль:** Случайно большие, средние и пустые видео вместе дают игровой эффект слот-машины; непредсказуемая награда сильнее предсказуемой.

> Unrewarding experience but the surprising thing is that those unrewarding videos the ones that you don't really care about and you just scroll past very quickly those are actually key to making tick tock as addictive as it is you see if you watch my tinder video from last week you'll remember that variable rewards are more rewarding than predictable ones every time you do that swiping motion to go to the next video on tick tock you're gambling as to whether the next video is going to be rewarding or not it might be one of those really amateur videos that are pretty rubbish that isn't rewarding or it could be one of those really cool videos that kind of blows your mind but the fact that the reward of the next video is unpredictable is what actually makes tick tock so addictive once again we have to make that classic comparison to the slot machines in las vegas swiping on tick tock is equivalent to cranking the handle on the slot machine you don't know whether you're going to win big or whether you're going to lose and the only way to find out is to keep cranking that handle.

**Для Напарника:** Редкие находки (аврора, редкий ориентир) как отклик на событие, но без слот-механики и бесконечного цикла.



# 🎬 Tiny Voices [Season 3, Episode 1] - Game Feel — Scribe


### Метафора: ожидания от привычных образов определяют, каким должно быть ощущение  ·  [12:11](https://youtu.be/Q1CQxc7Bmvo?t=731)
**Мысль:** Люди приносят схемы из жизни и других медиа: звук выстрела, отдача, спорткар быстрый, танк неповоротливый. Нарушение схемы воспринимается как неправильное.

> Moving on to sn's fifth pillar metaphor which might be better phrased as maybe schema or stereotype it's our understanding of real world analoges or stere types which inform our expectations of how things should handle or feel in game for instance even if you've never fired a gun you have a schema comble together from other media of how a gunshot is supposed to sound how there's supposed to be recoil so you have an expectation for how firing a gun in a game is supposed to feel alternatively a sports car should handle a certain way it should be sleek and smooth and maneuverable and fast versus a tank which is slow and lumbering a sports car being slow and lumbering would be unacceptable even though a tank having those same.

**Для Напарника:** Кот должен вести себя и двигаться как настоящий кот (мягко, лениво, с потягиваниями), иначе пропадёт ощущение присутствия.



# 🎬 What are loops in game design? - Loops - Game Design Theory — Game Design with Michael


### Сессия может длиться секунды: цикл сведён до базовой механики  ·  [00:54](https://youtu.be/PMj8Q4ViKzs?t=54)
**Мысль:** В строительных играх сессия: открыть, собрать ресурсы, закрыть. Цикл «собрать, выбрать, поставить в очередь, уйти и вернуться по готовности».

> Base building is an interesting genre as a play session may involve opening the game collecting resources and then immediately closing the game, with such a session lasting as short as a few seconds. That is because base building games along with many mobile titles have distilled the game design concept of loops down to being the base mechanic of the game. Collect resources, look at what to build next, queue it to build, leave and come back when the building has completed.

**Для Напарника:** Допустить микросессии в Напарнике (открыл, увидел рост острова, ушёл) как полноценную ценность.


### Цикл Дэна Кука: ментальная модель, действие, правила, обратная связь  ·  [01:30](https://youtu.be/PMj8Q4ViKzs?t=90)
**Мысль:** Мозг использует ментальную модель, чтобы выбрать действие, система применяет правила и возвращает обратную связь, которую игрок встраивает в модель.

> Loops are a concept that can be found in the interaction design and learning fields. When the brain is interacting with something there is a process that it goes through in order to adapt to its surroundings. There are several different versions of this loop so we will be using the one proposed by Daniel Cooke of Spry Fox. The process looks like this. We start at the top where we have a store of information the brain already has. This is called the mental model. At the start of the game this is likely going to be pretty empty, although it may have common things like genre constants to draw upon. An example of a genre constant might be most first-person shooters have very similar controls. The brain uses this information to devise an action that may have an effect on the current situation and then it acts. The action in the case of a game is received by the system and run through the rules. This is the part where the game takes the action the player has taken and reacts to it with an output. This output is then fed back to the player as a response to what has happened after their action, also known as feedback. The player then receives this information, interprets it by referencing it with their current understanding and tries to fit the new feedback into their mental model. The process then repeats.

**Для Напарника:** Моделировать каждое взаимодействие Напарника как такой цикл и проверять, что игрок получает понятный отклик на своё действие.


### Циклы бывают разного размера: быстрые и медленные  ·  [05:09](https://youtu.be/PMj8Q4ViKzs?t=309)
**Мысль:** Игры состоят из быстрых частых и медленных редких циклов; быстрый: «могу ли я нажать кнопку», медленнее: освоение комбинации.

> Right, so even though that section of play was only something like 20 seconds it can be seen how the player will go through this loop of coming up with an idea, acting on it, waiting for the game to process that action and give them feedback and then applying this new information to their mental model. That was also a good example to show how loops come in different sizes. Games are made up of all sized loops. Again using terminology from Daniel cook games can have fast frequent loops, all the way out to slow infrequent loops. So a fast look might be can I press the button? Yes. I can cool add that I can stand on buttons to my mental model. A slightly slower loop is that if I mix dash with running at the right time, I can make it through the door.

**Для Напарника:** Разделить циклы Напарника по длине: тап и отклик (секунды), сессия (минуты), рост острова (дни).


### Линия восприятия: без обратной связи цикл ломается  ·  [06:42](https://youtu.be/PMj8Q4ViKzs?t=402)
**Мысль:** Игрок видит только то, что над линией восприятия. В Hidden Folks игроки не знали, что объекты сдвигаются, и разработчик добавил покачивание объекта, замыкающее цикл.

> As a last little piece of the model I'm going to add this line. This is what I'm going to call the perception line. Everything above the perception line is available information to the player, everything below the perception line is hidden. So from the player's perspective they don't understand what the system is doing, they just take actions and are waiting for feedback to see if their actions did anything. So if something isn't working in your game you can break the mechanic down into its loot components. Does the loop look like this? If you miss the feedback step the game might be processing the players actions but the player doesn't know whether the game is doing anything or not. Hidden folks is a game that works similar to Where's Wally books. In a GDC talk, the developer of hidden folks spoke about players not realizing you could slide things out of the way to find characters. This is because there wasn't any feedback for the user to interpret. The solution the developer implemented to fix this was if a player tapped on an object that could slide but then didn't slide it, the object would wiggle. This wiggle completes the loop by providing feedback for an action which allows the player to adjust their mental model.

**Для Напарника:** Любое нажатие в Напарнике должно давать видимый отклик (покачивание, изменение), иначе человек не понимает, сработало ли действие.


### Ведение за руку не строит ментальную модель  ·  [08:28](https://youtu.be/PMj8Q4ViKzs?t=508)
**Мысль:** В FFXV: A New Empire игрок получил подсказки следовать за рукой и повторять «квест, улучшение», но не понял, как системы связаны; ментальная модель не сформировалась.

> Another way to use loops is to assess whether your feedback is being interpreted into the mental model of the player correctly. This is some footage from Final Fantasy XV:A New Empire, the mobile game. It at first looks very similar to Clash of Clans. The way I ended up playing this game was that instead of the feedback telling me this building does this, information which I should add to my mental model to make informed decisions in the future. I found myself just following the hand around for half an hour without being taught very much about the game and how the system's interconnect. Once the game stopped hand-holding me my mental model didn't say this is what I should do next because it will help my empire, it was press quest. Upgrade building, repeat. At one point I trained a soldier from the training grounds. By the end I still had no idea what the purpose of that action was. My interpretation of the feedback didn't translate into helpful information in my mental model.

**Для Напарника:** Онбординг Напарника должен объяснять, зачем действие и что оно даёт острову, а не просто вести за руку.



# 🎬 What EXCEPTIONAL Onboarding Actually Does - Building Successful Apps — Philipp Lackner


### Туториал по требованию на ключевых 20% функций, а не показ всего на старте  ·  [13:39](https://youtu.be/kouFJkcLKAQ?t=819)
**Мысль:** Люди хотят сразу начать использовать приложение; жёсткие туториалы раздражают. Лучше показывать краткую подсказку в момент, когда человек впервые идёт к функции, и только для 20% функций, которыми пользуются 80%, если они неочевидны. Подсказки привязаны к функции, а не к одному флагу «видел».

> If you've personally ever skipped a tutorial, whatever form that tutorial is, be it a tutorial for game, be it a tutorial inside an app that explains all its features, if you've ever skipped that, then you know why. And you can be sure that your users will also skip it. The moment someone installs an app, they want to start using that app as soon as possible, rather than being forced into some kind of rigid onboarding flow they have to first click through. This is something that really frustrates a large amount of users. Still, you may want to instruct the user a little bit on how to work with given features, especially if you have some complex features. So this is definitely a type of onboarding that you can stick to, but the much better approach here is to make it on demand. Do not, when the user just goes to the first screen of your app, do not show them everything of your app, everything it can do. Show multiple pop-ups that the user just clicks away. But when the user is actually going to a certain feature, initiating certain action, like tracking meal, then you could say, "Okay, now just for that feature, give them a brief introduction. Hey, here you can enter the title, here is the description, this is optional, you don't need this, and here is in the end whatever food you've tracked." Just as an example. So just on demand, when the user is about to do something, so when they already say, "Hey, I want to now finally use the app for what it is intended for," then you can show them, "Hey, this is how it works." I also wouldn't blindly do this for every single feature, but I would stick to the Pareto principle here. So that means for the 20% of your app's features that are used by 80% of your app's users, I would add such an onboarding flow if using that feature is not obvious or if there are some maybe uncommon tips you can give the user that may not be obvious. So, such good onboarding flows with tutorial like onboarding that happens across sessions and not just in the first session. So, it's not like you show them a tutorial and then after having clicked through that, you flip an internal flag in your settings and they will never see that again, but you actually have it per feature.

**Для Напарника:** Мини-подсказка кота при первом входе на Мир или первом разделении задачи на шаги, а не тур при старте. Без всплывающих окон/тостов: подсказка как реплика в чате или инлайн.



# 🎬 What is a Gameplay Loop: Steamworld Dig 2 — The Game Analyst


### Три шага петли: действие, награда, предвкушение  ·  [02:34](https://youtu.be/mq4IFZeMFaM?t=154)
**Мысль:** Награда - основной источник мотивации, а предвкушение (новые инструменты) заставляет с нетерпением возвращаться к действию.

> Step one or the action is the thing that the players will be doing throughout the game which in this instance is digging for materials this is the meat of the game that is a focal point of it all step two decay the reward is how the player is rewarded for the work they put in this example the reward of money and less tangibly a bigger tunnel and progressing story are considered the rewards this will be the prime source for motivation for the player moving forward to continue playing the game the final step three is the anticipation the purpose of this step is to make players excited to get back to step one and start the cycle anew the example in this case is receiving new upgrades and equipment making players want to play with their new tools in the action step of the next cycle.

**Для Напарника:** После награды (рост острова) показать намёк на то, что станет возможным дальше, чтобы тянуло начать следующий Фокус.


### Сначала короткие петли, затем всё длиннее: быстрые первые уровни  ·  [03:27](https://youtu.be/mq4IFZeMFaM?t=207)
**Мысль:** Несколько коротких петель быстро вовлекают новичка и задают ожидания; каждая следующая длиннее, как уровни в RPG.

> Another thing brought by a gameplay loop is that the player can become invested in the game rather quickly if the loop is set up correctly a few short loops can help set up what newcomers can expect from the game with every successive loop increasing in length to encourage long-term play with more lengthier loops levels in rpg and any game that gets progressively harder are good examples of this idea as usually the first few level ups happen rapidly while the weight between the last few levels is rather lengthy.

**Для Напарника:** Первые визиты: быстрые награды после первой минуты и первой сессии; интервалы между наградами растут по мере привычки.


### Расписание наград: часто в начале, потом постепенно реже  ·  [06:11](https://youtu.be/mq4IFZeMFaM?t=371)
**Мысль:** Сначала награда за каждое действие закрепляет условную связь, потом интервалы удлиняются; игрок, привыкший к наградам, ждёт их, а длинные интервалы оставляют для опытных.

> One idea that is on full display in steamworld dig 2 is the length of time between rewards offer and conditioning could work well if the subject is rewarded every time the task is completed but often times it's more beneficial to encourage a waiting time between rewards video games as mentioned will start with you quickly gaining rewards to get the operant conditioning mindset in place and slowly lengthen the time between rewards this has the effect of making a smaller number of rewards last over a greater period of time this is only allowable as the subject has grown accustomed to being rewarded the length of every reward cycle was a long wait the player would not be as incentivized to continue the game as they do not have the subconscious mentality of knowing the reward is awaiting them the longer a player has been in the cycle the more likely they are to wait for a reward so the lengthier reward periods are saved for the few that trek towards the end of the game.

**Для Напарника:** Расписание наград острова: первые сессии награждают всегда, позже редкие находки; при возвращении снова щедро.


### Разбавляй петлю сменой темпа, но не чрезмерно  ·  [07:16](https://youtu.be/mq4IFZeMFaM?t=436)
**Мысль:** Секции, намеренно идущие против петли (головоломки), снимают монотонность, но должны быть небольшой частью игры.

> But the last thing a game should do is allow for the loop to become stale and no quicker way does that than pure repetition sure a player might take breaks and allow the game to be enticing from beginning to end but how do you do that for players that are playing continuously at length one way is to introduce sections of game that purposely go against the loop using steam world dig 2 as an example the puzzle platforming in particular give a nice change of pace to allow for the loop to not get monotonous and tedious as they require a slower methodical approach to that of digging these can even have their own rewards and gameplay loops too the important thing to note here is that these side distractions should only be a relatively small size of the game as the core gameplay loop should still be the primary focus.

**Для Напарника:** Иногда предлагать необычный формат (например, короткий ритуал вместо таймера), оставляя Фокус главным.


### Петля должна эволюционировать: добавляй по одному элементу  ·  [08:08](https://youtu.be/mq4IFZeMFaM?t=488)
**Мысль:** Начни просто и постепенно добавляй элементы: игра остаётся интересной, потому что час 1 не похож на час 30.

> Though just having a core gameplay loop often times isn't enough to avoid repetition it is important for this core gameplay loop to evolve and expand with the player in steamworld dig the loop starts fairly simple with just you and your trusty shovel before it quickly evolves to have grappling hooks pneumatic fists and a slew of other gadgets to expand the player's abilities new enemies environments and challenges also aim to evolve the gameplay loop it's important the game starts simple for a player to learn and gradually add in an element at a time to increase the complexity and involvement in the loop slowly adding in a single element at a time also allows a game to remain engaging deep into the experience since if every aspect was there from the beginning hour one would feel a lot like hour 30.

**Для Напарника:** Прогрессивное раскрытие: новые возможности появляются постепенно по мере использования, а не сразу.



# 🎬 What is body doubling? How do you Body Double? | Learn to Thrive with ADHD | Adult ADHD Coaching — ADHD Coach Mande John - Learn to Thrive with ADHD


### Сессии 25/50 минут с встроенными перерывами 5/10  ·  [00:55](https://youtu.be/Mevza749I44?t=55)
**Мысль:** Back-to-back сессии получают автоматическую паузу — подвигаться, попить, сходить в туалет.

> You can do 25 minute sessions or 50 minute sessions. And what's really cool is if you do sessions back to back, you actually have little breaks kind of built in. So if you're doing your 50 minute sessions back to back, you have a 10 minute break. If you're doing the 25 minute, you. A five minute break before the next one starts. So that's kind of nice. You can get up, move around, get a drink of water, have a bio break. That kind of thing.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус: 25 мин по умолчанию и встроенный перерыв, который предлагается сразу после финала.



# 🎬 What Makes a Game Cozy? ~ Design Doc — Design Doc


### Ключи уюта: низкое давление, простота, персонажи, лёгкий тон  ·  [02:04](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=124)
**Мысль:** Без последствий за провал и без сложного выживания, простые стилизованные визуалы, симпатичные персонажи, лёгкий сюжет; можно вообще без сюжета.

> I think the keys to coziness are low-pressure gameplay, without a lot of consequences for failing at a task or difficult survival elements, simple, stylistic visuals, fun characters, and a light story tone. You could even get away with basically no story if you want.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-лист тона Напарника: без последствий за пропуск, простые формы, милые персонажи, лёгкие реплики.


### Нет заданного пути: ключевые предметы разбросаны в нескольких местах  ·  [03:10](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=190)
**Мысль:** Цели можно достигать в любом порядке, а предмет найдется в нескольких местах, поэтому прогресс возникает в ходе того, что интересно человеку; ничто не закрыто.

> As you go from place to place in the park, you’ll stumble into something new around almost every corner. You’ll find key items scattered throughout, unlocking new mechanics to use, but the game doesn’t limit you to only one place to find the items. These buds bloom and act as a trampoline to let you glide around easier, but the bucket you need to activate them can be found in a bunch of places throughout the mountain. Not just one. There’s a kid that needs a shovel to finish his sandcastle, but there are 5 different places where the game will let you find one. Scattering key items like that helps you complete tasks in the middle of doing whatever exploring you found most interesting. There is no set path up the hill and no set order in which you’ll meet people or complete tasks. It’s entirely at your own pace, and you can take or leave the elements that most interest you. You don’t have to complete everything either, there’s nothing really to gate you off from achieving your goals, save for one clearly marked place, and you just collect what you want to in whatever order feels right. Whatever you want to do is totally fine with the game.

**Для Напарника:** Дать несколько путей к одной цели на острове и в очереди шагов, чтобы начинать можно было с того, что тянет.


### Падение без штрафа и с поддержкой  ·  [04:11](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=251)
**Мысль:** Нет наказания за промах, а у птицы есть планирование для выхода из ситуации; ошибка воспринимается как нормальная часть исследования.

> Aw, you fell off a cliff while exploring? Well, that’s very good… for… a first… try. You keep at it! There’s no real penalty for missing a jump or gliding too far or falling off a cliff. You’re a bird, after all, so you can glide your way out of most things anyway.

**Для Напарника:** Срыв сессии или пропуск дня в Напарнике должен иметь «планер» в виде мягкого возврата и тёплой фразы кота.


### Смотровые точки показывают, как далеко ты зашёл  ·  [04:35](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=275)
**Мысль:** Дымка на среднем расстоянии и обесцвечивание далёкого дают глубину и намёк на то, куда идти дальше; на вершине видно пройденный путь.

> The sense of depth that the game plays with is outstanding. From the game’s usual isometric angle, you don’t get a sense of it. You’re focused on the local area and what you can do, and where you can go. But every so often, you’ll reach one of these lookout points, and you can see just how far you’ve come. There’s a haze effect at medium range and a really smart desaturation and outline technique the game uses for things far off in the distance, hinting at something that might pique your interest. Maybe you should go over there, too?

**Для Напарника:** Мир/AppBackdrop: гряда на горизонте как дымчатая даль, а экран Мира как смотровая точка, показывающая пройденное.


### Свой темп: задержаться или рвануть, оба варианта верны  ·  [05:07](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=307)
**Мысль:** Игра принимает и медленное общение со всеми, и быстрый подъём на гору: это ваш отпуск.

> Or not, whatever you’re feeling. The whole game might only take you an hour or two to complete, but that’s really up to you. If you want to linger around talking to everyone you find and helping them with their problems, be my guest. If you want to blitz up the mountain, have at it. It’s your getaway. Do whatever feels right to you.

**Для Напарника:** Позволять Напарнику вести и спокойные 10 минут, и 50 минут, без оценки темпа.


### Уют и ясная цель совместимы  ·  [05:34](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=334)
**Мысль:** Grow Home имеет одну чёткую цель, но остаётся расслабленным: нет времени давления, нет боя, падение почти ничего не стоит, подбадривает бортовой компьютер, и можно останавливаться на видах.

> Games tend to feel cozier when they have low consequences, but that doesn’t mean they have to be these squishy, aimless things. They can still feel cozy while being focused and having clear goals. Grow Home is a very simple physics-based platformer. You’re a clumsy little robot named BUD who has to jump, climb, and glide his way around a huge plant to get back to his mothership floating high above the planet. The plant is filled with these branching buds. If you grab one, you’ll ride it as it grows and you can sort of steer the plant towards other things, like these glowing landmasses that give energy to the plant. Along the way you’ll find and collect some crystals that give BUD upgrades that help you traverse the world easier. That’s about it. Grow Home has a very single-minded overarching goal, but stays cozy with its relaxing cycle of jumping, climbing, growing plants, and collecting as you get closer and closer to the mothership. It’s more single-minded than A Short Hike, but it’s still very relaxed and lets you go about that goal at your own pace. The game uses a calm, ambient soundtrack, a low-poly art style, the occasional soothing song, and the mothership’s computer cheering you on to keep it’s relaxed vibe. There’s no time pressure, no combat, and hardly any enemies to get in your way, save for the occasional creature. Even if you died to it, though, there’s not much consequence, or much consequence from falling off of the plant for that matter. You’ll just get teleported back to the nearest beacon. It’s just you, the plant, and a ton of crystals to collect as you climb your way back home. But as you go up, pause for a second every once in a while and take in a vista. You’ve earned it.

**Для Напарника:** Цель сессии может быть чёткой (один шаг), а окружение расслабленным; кот подбадривает, а не оценивает.


### Уют не обязан быть сладким  ·  [07:10](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=430)
**Мысль:** Untitled Goose Game уютна, хотя вы злодей: минимальный саундтрек, плоские цвета, возможность пробовать на NPC, почти нет шанса провала, легко вернуться.

> A cozy game doesn’t have to be sugary sweet, either. A Short Hike and Animal Crossing are known for being as pleasant as possible all the way through, but you can still have a nice, low-key time being an absolute jerk. It worked for Dennis the Menace, why can’t it work for a goose? Untitled Goose Game is a cozy game where you play as the villain. You’re a goose working your way through a small neighborhood and going through a literal checklist of mayhem, ruining the day of everyone in your path. It sounds mean-spirited, and it is, but it’s still so cozy and endearing. The presentation and scope of the game do a lot of work here. It’s a puzzle-focused adventure game where you operate on the world by manipulating NPCs to complete tasks. It’s all about exploring the different ways you can affect the environment and mess around with the townsfolk in specific ways. Actually, it’s kinda like a cozy version of Hitman if you think about it. Honk at this kid over and over and you’ll scare him. Chase him around and you might trap him in the phonebooth. Or you could steal this gardener’s prized rose and while he’s distracted, you can snatch his hat and force him to wear THIS. There’s plenty to poke at in the environment and lots of things to experiment on to see what different behaviors everyone will have in different scenarios. Like A Short Hike, there’s basically no chance to fail and it’s easy to get right back to where you were if something goes weird. The minimalist piano soundtrack, flat colored art style and the premise all lend themselves to a delightful low-key experience of being an absolute nuisance.

**Для Напарника:** У кота может быть озорной характер, не обязательно только милый; важны низкие ставки и лёгкий возврат.


### Эстетика не делает игру уютной: Night in the Woods  ·  [08:39](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=519)
**Мысль:** Игра выглядит уютной, но тяжёлые темы упадка и перемен выводят её из категории. Уют — результат согласованных факторов, а не только внешнего вида.

> You might think you see a pattern here. Lots of cozy games seem to have fairly similar aesthetics, but be careful. Focusing on aesthetics to determine whether or not a game is cozy can trip you up. On the surface, Night in the Woods looks extremely cozy. Look at that art style. Animal characters. The dialogue is super casual and relatable. The music’s great. It’s not mechanically demanding. It’s just this cozy little adventure game where you go around your small town and do stuff, right? Oh. Oh no. Well yeah sort of. But no. Night in the Woods’ story I’d say takes it firmly out of the ‘cozy’ camp. You play as Mae, who’s coming home from college under… mixed circumstances. Your hometown is there, but nothing’s quite how you left it. There’s the natural change over time, especially in that post-high school way where all your friends drift apart a little bit to do their own thing. But the town’s not doing so hot, either. Work is hard to come by and the town is in this slow decay cycle. The people in it are a little more standoffish than you remember. It’s all sorta the same, but not… quite. You’re gonna meet back up with your best friends, catch up, and see if there’s something else going on. The game explores personal growth and change, urban decay, social and psychological trauma, and whether or not you CAN go home again. It’s not a HUGE downer, but these are some heavy themes that the game plays up.

**Для Напарника:** Не полагаться только на милую визуалку: тон текстов и механик должен соответствовать уютному обещанию.


### Уют — продукт множества согласованных факторов  ·  [10:32](https://youtu.be/g4yT-uAUcSA?t=632)
**Мысль:** Уютная игра не только эстетика, она результат совместной работы множества факторов, направленных на расслабление.

> Cozy games aren’t just about an aesthetic, they’re the product of lots of factors working together to help you relax.

**Для Напарника:** Рассматривать звук, цвет, тексты, механику и ритм Напарника как единую систему уюта.



# 🎬 What Makes A Good Game Companion? — Design Doc


### Навязчивый спутник (Navi, Fi) против тихого сигнала (Tatl) и подсказок по запросу  ·  [02:43](https://youtu.be/pfNzYJ0S8Oo?t=163)
**Мысль:** Navi заговаривает каждые 20 секунд; Tatl привлекает внимание колокольчиком; Fi прерывает действие очевидными подсказками. Исправление: подсказки по запросу, оптимальные для темпа игры.

> Probably the most common use of a sidekick is as a way to give information to the player in-universe. No data logs and text boxes, just your friend telling you what's up. The Zelda fairies in the two N64 games are the classic vanilla examples, for better and worse. Navi in Ocarina of Time and Tatl in Majora's Mask are primarily there to guide the player in various ways. That can be contextualizing the world, giving objectives, lending hints on how to get past enemies or puzzles, highlighting points of interest, and especially providing tutorials for new game concepts like Z-Targeting. They do their jobs well, though they're especially notorious for how overbearing they can be. Navi is infamous for chiming in every twenty seconds about something, even things you've dealt with before. Tatl reigned it in to be less intrusive. She doesn’t pop up as often and instead of yelling out annoying voice lines, she gets your attention with a little bell chime that doesn’t hit a nerve nearly as much. Most of the later Zelda companions kept driving in a less intrusive direction and got more utility added on top, but that all went away with Skyward Sword. Fi is Navi EX Ultra Savage. Everything people didn’t like about Navi is ramped up with Fi. The original release of Skyward Sword is extremely hand-holdy, with Fi interrupting the action all the time to give obvious gameplay tips and restate mission objectives and info the player JUST heard, or saw. Fi is explicitly written as a cold and calculating robot, which leaves little else to save her character. It’s tough to know when a sidekick should chime in, but that doesn’t mean that the answer is ‘chime in all the time’. The interruptions destroy the pace of the game for no benefit. The HD re-release drastically toned down Fi’s hints and made most of them optional, where the player could call on her when they wanted the info, which by itself fixes most of the problem and made Skyward Sword, if not great, at least much less of a chore to play.

**Для Напарника:** Реплики кота во время сессии: редкий мягкий сигнал, а подсказки только по запросу; соответствует метафоре присутствия, не надзора.


### Бесполезный компаньон так же плох: непоследовательная система подсказок равна её отсутствию  ·  [04:32](https://youtu.be/pfNzYJ0S8Oo?t=272)
**Мысль:** Если спутник нужен, чтобы выйти из тупика, он должен надёжно помогать, когда его зовут.

> You might think the fix is to crank down how helpful your sidekick is. If people don’t like a backseat driver, just do the opposite, right? Be careful what you wish for. It can be just as frustrating to have a borderline-useless companion character - someone who has basically nothing to say when you directly ask them for help. Paper Mario: Sticker Star has many problems, and… oh wait, I’ve gotta do something first. OK, Sticker Star has Kersti, a magical… sticker fairy who needs you to collect a bunch of sticker MacGuffins from Bowser. She’s got some light sticker power gimmicks, but she’s mostly there as a hint system, which this game definitely needs. Sticker Star isn’t that different from an old-school Sierra-style point-and-click adventure game with a lot of unintuitive, hyper-specific puzzle solutions It’s easy to get stuck. Thank God Kersti’s here to get us back on track. Hit me up with that sweet advice Kersti… oh come on. Kersti doesn’t consistently work as a hint system. Often when you call on her, she’ll say some fluff about the area, or she’ll say that the thing you need is somewhere. Thanks, Kersti. No consistency is about as useful as no hint system at all. She MIGHT be more specific or might tell you where you can find what you need, but only if you’ve had the item you need already, otherwise you get nothing. Sometimes it’s the opposite for some reason. If you HAD the right piece of the puzzle in the past but don't have it now, she won't give you any real hint as to what you might've needed. Good luck! Hint systems can be a great responsibility for a sidekick to take care of, but if that’s the mechanism to get out of a jam, you really should make sure they can deliver in your hour of need.

**Для Напарника:** Когда человек спрашивает кота «что делать», ответ всегда конкретный шаг, а не вода.


### Спутник как живая часть интерфейса (метроном, здоровье, курсор)  ·  [06:30](https://youtu.be/pfNzYJ0S8Oo?t=390)
**Мысль:** Совпадение ролей персонажа и UI естественно: оба ведут игрока к нужному.

> Stretch the idea a little further, and you can get a sidekick that’s basically a living piece of the user interface. 808 in Hi Fi Rush is a living metronome - well, along with the rest of the world - and can help you stay on and get back to the beat to perform more effective attacks and defensive maneuvers. Sparx in the Spyro series represents your health, can pick up nearby collectibles, and even points you in the direction of where to get more. In Phantom Hourglass, THIS Zelda fairy is the literal cursor you control on the touch screen, and Link will blindly follow wherever it goes. It might seem weird for a character to stand in for pieces of an interface, but they’re both supposed to work to guide you to what you need to do. The overlapping responsibility makes it a surprisingly natural fit.

**Для Напарника:** Кот как индикатор состояния сессии (дыхание, поза) вместо отдельного прогресс-бара.


### Ключевая механика привязана к партнёру; его возврат даёт настоящее облегчение  ·  [07:14](https://youtu.be/pfNzYJ0S8Oo?t=434)
**Мысль:** FLUDD и Cappy лишь временно забирают, и без них ощущается нехватка; возврат даёт глубокое облегчение.

> We can go beyond a guidebook and UI, though. Sidekicks can be the source of a lot of the player’s core abilities. A central gameplay mechanic might be tied directly to what the partner can do or can provide. Mario Sunshine has FLUDD, a robotic water pack that can spray water for attacking enemies, cleaning goop, or for moving around the world. The water mechanics were the big new thing added to separate the game from Mario 64, and what better way to teach you how to do them than to have your partner just tell you? Mario Odyssey's hat mechanic is its main draw, so slap some googly eyes on your cap, give it some dialogue and now you’ve got a partner. Cappy gives you tons of more movement and attack options. You can throw it forward, use it as a stepping stone to jump off of, and most importantly, possess the mind of whoever you see. It acts as a typical guide, too, giving out hints and showing you points of interest as you travel through the world. It feels a little like visiting all of these places with a friend. But sometimes you don’t know what you’re missing until it’s gone. Both Cappy and FLUDD are briefly taken away from you over the course of the game, and not having them is immediately felt. The movement mechanics they’re responsible for are fundamental to most of the puzzles and challenges the games throw at you, and without those mid-air movement options, you’re stuck with just your core Mario platforming abilities. It gets a lot tougher to move around, especially in Mario Sunshine’s case. The relief you feel when you get FLUDD and Cappy back is real, and it’s on a deeper level than just missing them being around.

**Для Напарника:** Привязать механику роста острова к коту. Не применимо: намеренно забирать кота как наказание.


### Контраст характеров: молчаливому герою нужен собеседник с отличным характером  ·  [09:52](https://youtu.be/pfNzYJ0S8Oo?t=592)
**Мысль:** Когда Jak обрёл личность, диалог с Daxter стал живым; роль спутника проявляется в контрасте.

> Sidekicks can also help other character’s personalities shine through, just from how the sidekick contrasts with others. As a character, Jak in the original Jak and Daxter is barely anything. He plays the straight man without saying much at all. A classic silent protagonist. His partner Daxter is a wisecracking Looney Tune. But since Jak isn’t going to pipe up here, Daxter is left to do more of the story’s leg work than he could honestly handle. It’s a little easier though when your companion has another personality to play off of. Daxter has to rely on the game’s other NPCs to do that job. He’ll snark his way through a plot conversation to help move things along while Jak mostly just stands there. A shoulder shrug here and there is most of what you get, but it’s not much to build a character off of. Daxter becomes a bit overbearing as a comic relief sidekick with no one to consistently play off of, and Jak ends up being completely forgettable as a protagonist. The duo needed an overhaul. As the series went on and drastically changed in tone, Naughty Dog felt that Jak needed his own personality, and, like, dialogue. In Jak 2, Jak became a more grounded and serious character for Daxter to bounce off of, and now he could bring moments of levity to a tonally darker game. both of them became stronger characters thanks to the contrast. The banter becomes more real, as the characters can rib on each other and Jak can properly act as the straight man to Daxter’s antics rather than just standing around. The game lampshades the change in Jak’s personality too, so the writers knew exactly what they were trying to fix. The new ability for the two to play off of one another helps sell the audience on their friendship and lets Daxter be more of an actual sidekick rather than an overburdened character.

**Для Напарника:** У кота должен быть узнаваемый характер, отличающийся от тона пользователя.


### Спутник, который теплеет со временем, даёт полезные, но ненавязчивые подсказки и удобные «хуки»  ·  [16:26](https://youtu.be/pfNzYJ0S8Oo?t=986)
**Мысль:** Постепенное сближение, умеренные подсказки, бытовые удобства (телепорт) и совместная атака укрепляют связь между героем и спутником.

> Her warming up to you over the hours doesn’t make her any less of a fun character either. By the time you get to face off against Zant for real, she doesn’t pull any punches. Plus, as a partner, she’s helpful, providing decent but not overbearing hints, quality of life hooks like teleporting you and objects around, as well as giving a fun team-up attack to help build a stronger bond between the two of you.

**Для Напарника:** Кот постепенно становится теплее и ближе по мере сессий; микро-полезности вместо навязчивых подсказок.



# 🎬 What makes the perfect cozy game? — Adam Plays


### Три столпа уюта: безопасность, стиль, побег  ·  [01:51](https://youtu.be/_fhLF0CH-WQ?t=111)
**Мысль:** Безопасность значит не отсутствие плохого, а то, что тебя не накажут за неправильную игру: игра доверяет игроку, ошибки обратимы.

> Well, I've put the core design of cozy games into three main pillars: safety, style, and escape. At its core, a cozy game usually makes the player feel safe. Not safe as in nothing bad ever happens and you will never fail and never die, but more safe as in you're not going to be punished for playing the wrong way. The game should trust the player and give them the freedom to play how they want to, with mistakes feeling recoverable rather than devastating.

**Для Напарника:** Три столпа как чек-лист для Напарника: ошибки и пропуски обратимы (безопасность), единый тёплый стиль, пространство без давления.


### Обратимый провал: последствие есть, но предсказуемое и не фатальное  ·  [03:41](https://youtu.be/_fhLF0CH-WQ?t=221)
**Мысль:** В Stardew потеря сознания даёт небольшой денежный штраф, а не сброс дня; в Mario Odyssey падение стоит несколько монет. Игра признаёт ошибку, применяет последствие и позволяет продолжать.

> One of the best ways to create a sense of security is to make failure feel recoverable. You should never feel like you made a mistake that requires you to reload a save or even restart the game or run entirely. The game should, of course, acknowledge the mistake, apply some sort of consequence, and then let you keep moving on. A pretty great example of this would be, of course, Stardew Valley. And don't worry, I'll have some more unique examples, I promise. I cannot make this another video just about Stardew. When you pass out at 2:00 a.m. or in the mines, the game doesn't reset your day or delete your progress. Instead, it applies a small money penalty based on your total amount of money and maybe removes a few items. The loss exists, but it's predictable and never fatal to your save. You can even buy back some of the lost items if you got really unlucky. You learn to manage your time better, but the game never makes you feel punished for learning slowly.

**Для Напарника:** Если человек бросил сессию или пропустил день, последствие мягкое и предсказуемое (ноль, а не минус), без сброса прогресса острова.


### Доверие к праву на ошибку освобождает любопытство  ·  [05:07](https://youtu.be/_fhLF0CH-WQ?t=307)
**Мысль:** Обучение через ошибку - универсальный индикатор хорошего дизайна. Когда страх провала уходит, игрок экспериментирует и вовлекается эмоционально, а не механически.

> But recoverability alone can be enhanced by actually teaching players with their failure. A great example of this is Slime Rancher, personally a like top three cozy game for me, I think. You can lose slimes and mess up plort economies, but the game never hard resets your progress. These mistakes do cost time and efficiency, but they allow you to learn optimal setups and gameplay mechanics naturally through trial and error. And if I'm being honest, teaching through failure is pretty much a universal good game design indicator. It's great for cozy games, but also great for all sorts of other games, too. Either way, both of these are important to build trust with the game. Once you trust that you're allowed to make mistakes and learn from them, you as a player can start experimenting and engaging with the game emotionally rather than mechanically. And that's exactly when the game starts to feel cozy. >> Once the fear of failure is gone, you can play more with curiosity, and that's a lot more interesting and relaxing to play with.

**Для Напарника:** Онбординг и Фокус: показывать, что сбой шага нормален, и тем самым разрешать эксперименты с длительностью и шагами.


### Звук уюта: зацикленная музыка, мягкие приятные эффекты, тишина и амбиент  ·  [06:34](https://youtu.be/_fhLF0CH-WQ?t=394)
**Мысль:** Музыка должна зацикливаться и не надоедать; эффекты мягкие и удовлетворяющие; сигнал при завершении участка делает нудное приятным. Тишина и амбиент создают ощущение природы.

> Cozy games tend to use music that loops naturally and doesn't get annoying after you've heard it a million times. Tracks are memorable, but they don't overstay their welcome. Furthermore, sound effects are soft, and most importantly to me at least, satisfying. >> Think of some of the most memorable and nostalgic game soundtracks of all time. Minecraft, Animal Crossing, even Plants vs. Zombies. There's a reason I and so many other YouTubers use these songs as background music. Everyone just loves them. You can listen to them countless times during the game and not get bored. And sound effects matter just as much. All the games I previously mentioned have great, memorable sounds, but even something like PowerWash Simulator is perfect. It's built around satisfaction through slow progress and the steady sound of water and slight audio cue when you finish a section really makes this boring task that much cozier. But something that can also work great for cozy audio design is just silence, or ambience as well. Sometimes stepping back and just letting the player experience the world and the sounds within it really let you get comfortable and almost feel like you're out in nature, which for a lot of people, including myself, is very relaxing.

**Для Напарника:** Звук костра в ambient.ts - верное направление; редкие мягкие сигналы на событиях, а не на каждом касании.


### Побег: игрок сам задаёт скорость, нет срочности и наказания за паузу  ·  [11:04](https://youtu.be/_fhLF0CH-WQ?t=664)
**Мысль:** Уют создаёт пространство, где время замедляется и давление уходит; важнее всего контроль. Необязательные цели вместо принудительных, общая цель лишь фон в голове.

> It's more about giving the player a space where time slows down, pressure fades, and the outside world just feels a little bit farther away. And the most important part of that is control. There are a lot of ways games can do this. The first of which is quite simply letting the player set the speed at which they play. There shouldn't be a constant sense of urgency. You shouldn't be rushing from objective to objective and you're not punished for just stopping and honestly doing nothing for a while. The world should kind of progress as you do. This can be done with things like optional objectives instead of forced ones. Maybe you have the overall goal from the very beginning, defeat Calamity Ganon, restore the community center, escape the planet. But this is kind of just a motivator in the back of your mind at most times. You're playing just for the sake of playing. You're experiencing and escaping to this virtual world.

**Для Напарника:** Фокус: длительность выбирает человек, нет сигнала срочности; план на день - мягкий фон, а не обязанность.


### Короткие сессии: прогресс маленькими осмысленными кусками  ·  [11:58](https://youtu.be/_fhLF0CH-WQ?t=718)
**Мысль:** Даже за несколько минут можно что-то завершить; побег не должен ощущаться обязательством и должен быть доступен тем, у кого мало времени.

> One of the most underrated aspects of cozy game design is how well it supports short play sessions. Cozy games understand that their players might only have a few minutes to play every day. Mainly because they are crafted with a more casual gaming audience in mind. So they design progress in small, meaningful chunks. >> This might seem to clash with the last point, but if you don't have the time to fully immerse yourself in the world every time you play, you can still accomplish something during every session. And you can see this in so many of the games I've mentioned thus far. Stardew Valley has that take about 15 minutes each, plenty of time to do tons of important tasks. Plants vs. Zombies is split into short levels. Same goes for Donut County. Pikmin also follows the each level is a day form of progression. Even a game like The Talos Principle, which is honestly a pretty odd cozy game, is great to just pick up and beat a puzzle or two and then continue along with your day. And this is very important because escape shouldn't feel like a commitment. It should feel accessible to everyone, even those who only have a short amount of time to play each day.

**Для Напарника:** Короткие фокус-сессии (5-15 мин) каждая приносит видимый прирост на острове, никаких долгов за пропуск.


### Прогресс виден как изменение мира, а не рост статистики  ·  [13:05](https://youtu.be/_fhLF0CH-WQ?t=785)
**Мысль:** Вместо подсчёта чисел игрок видит, как мир меняется, и строит его по-своему, а персонализация делает игру домом.

> Exceptionally well is make progress visual instead of purely numerical. This fits in pretty well with the style pillar as well, but basically instead of watching stats go up, you watch the world change. You can build something yourself, exactly how you like it, really making the game feel more like home. You guys might be mad at me for this one, but I have personally never played The Sims, but I've seen so many people call it cozy, and it's literally built around this entire concept, I think. I think that's like all you do in this game. You create a house, an entire family, exactly how you like it. And this is seen in most cozy games that have any sort of crafting and survival mechanics, like Minecraft, Stardew Valley, Valheim, even Subnautica. Personalization goes a long way in making a cozy game.

**Для Напарника:** Остров как визуальный прогресс; дать возможность персонализации (выбор, где что стоит, имя) для чувства дома.


### Что ломает уют: гринд, стрессовые системы реального времени, сложность  ·  [15:06](https://youtu.be/_fhLF0CH-WQ?t=906)
**Мысль:** Слишком много гринда, стрессовых real-time систем и переусложнения разрушают уют; уют не равен медленному.

> And this is where a lot of games that want to be cozy tend to fail. It's a relatively new concept in the game world, or at least a much more popular one now that gaming has such a larger market, but mistakes like too much grind or stressful real-time systems, or overly complex systems can really bring a possibly great cozy experience down a lot. Just the most important thing to keep in mind is that cozy is not the same as slow.

**Для Напарника:** Перечень антипаттернов для проверки каждой новой фичи Напарника: нет гринда, нет таймеров реального времени, нет сложности.



# 🎬 What Pokémon Did To Real Animals — Playlosophy


### Механика дружбы: игра вознаграждает за доброту к существу  ·  [02:58](https://youtu.be/w_qmsjD9WnU?t=178)
**Мысль:** Стат дружбы растёт от прогулок, ягод, боёв без поражения и открывает эволюцию; позже добавили поглаживания, угощения, лагерь, пикники.

> Generation 2 was the first leap the games made in incorporating Pokémon welfare as a mechanic. They introduced friendship, a stat that went up when you walked with a Pokémon, fed it berries, used it in battle without it fainting. Some Pokémon could only evolve when this number was high enough, which meant the game was now mechanically rewarding you for being kind. Then in Generation 6, we got Pokémon Amie, where you could pet your Pokémon on the touch screen, feed them treats, play mini-games with them. By Gen 8, you were camping with them. By Gen 9, you were having picnics.

**Для Напарника:** Рост острова и расцвет кота могут быть наградой за заботу и присутствие (неагрессивные действия), без штрафа за отсутствие.


### Покемоны «теряют сознание», а не умирают; бесплатное лечение без вопросов  ·  [04:36](https://youtu.be/w_qmsjD9WnU?t=276)
**Мысль:** Слово fainted задаёт ожидание восстановления; Покецентр бесплатно и без вопросов лечит всю команду: цикл «бой-лечение-бой», вред обратим при заботе.

> But here's the thing, Pokémon do not die. Yes, in the lore of the series, Pokémon are confirmed multiple times to experience death, but in a battle, even if a small mammal is struck down by a god ray from a literal deity, when a Pokémon's HP hits zero, the game does not say your Rattata was killed, it says it has fainted. A medical word that implies the harm is temporary and that recovery is the expectation. This is not a small choice. Most other RPGs on the market in 1996 had their enemies die. Final Fantasy enemies died. Dragon Quest enemies died. Even your party members died and had to be revived at churches with literal religious resurrection mechanics. Pokémon alone decided that violence in this universe would have a ceiling, and the ceiling was unconsciousness, treatable in 10 seconds for free at the nearest clinic. That clinic is the Pokémon Center, a building in every single Pokémon game ever made. A building where you can heal your entire team to full health for free with no questions asked. Nurse Joy is always there, and not once does she ever check your health insurance. The Pokémon Center is the most frictionless universal health care system in the history of fiction. And let's just appreciate how strange it is that a Japanese corporation franchise has been quietly telling an entire generation of children that medical care should be free and instant. Because of the Pokémon Center, the game's loop is not fight, fight, fight. It is fight, heal, fight. A battle is never truly over until the animal is whole again. And the takeaway lodged in player's brain is that harm to animals is real, but it is reversible if you respond to it with care.

**Для Напарника:** Прямая модель для тона Напарника: срыв сессии или пропуск = «передохнули», возврат без вины и штрафов; без регрессии острова.



# 🎬 What's your game's core loop? — Ivan Alexiev Game Design


### Петля создаёт предвкушение: работа сначала тяжёлая, потом работает на тебя  ·  [02:09](https://youtu.be/TzSp47iEFh8?t=129)
**Мысль:** В Splendor сначала много усилий, потом карты работают за тебя, и игрок чувствует прогресс. Повторяющаяся петля даёт планировать и ждать, а план и его исполнение делают награды лучше.

> At first, you'll do a lot of taking gems actions, but as you get more cards, you don't need as many gems, so the ratio is going to change. And so, you're going to feel that feeling of progress. And you're going to have players have in their heads this question of, oh, am I at some point going to get cards for free? And yes, you will. And that's part of like the game structure and what makes the game fun is at first you've got to work really hard to get what you want and then you don't need to work as hard because your cards are working for you. One thing that the core game loop or a loop in general does for games is it can build anticipation. And you can see that actually really well in games with rounds. So, when you have a round, typically, how often are you going to tell players, "Hey, let's do one full round and then you're going to get the game." Well, that happens because you do the same things over and over again. And once you do them once, then players start planning. They start anticipating what's going to happen next round. And the game suddenly gets interesting. You're not going to be doing different actions every single time. You're going to be doing the same actions, but it's going to you're going to be changing the context of those actions. you're going to be changing the power of those actions and those actions are going to be affecting each other which is a big part of what makes games fun. And so game loops can build anticipation because players can see, oh, I'm going to buy a card now and then later that card is going to come which might help me in this other part of the loop which is maybe combat like in Dune Imperium for instance. And then if I win at combat, then that's gonna make buying cards easier because I'm gonna get spice and then I'm going to get that other thing. And so players can start anticipating. They can start planning. One of the types of fun is making a plan and then executing it or making a plan and seeing if it's going to execute. Is great and it makes rewards all the.

**Для Напарника:** Ранние сессии заметно дороже усилием, а накопленное на острове со временем начинает приносить само: чувство прогресса и ожидания.


### Пейсинг: напряжение и разрядка, постепенное усложнение петли даёт ещё один ход  ·  [05:13](https://youtu.be/TzSp47iEFh8?t=313)
**Мысль:** Хороший темп чередует напряжение и отдых. В Civilization петля растёт шаг за шагом, и уже к концу хочется повторить начало.

> You don't want cinematic scene after cinematic scene after cinematic scene or combat after combat after combat. For good pacing, you want a na natural like tension and then a release of tension and then tension then a release of tension. Or you can see that as action and then rest. Action and then rest. when speaking of pacing, the most famous example of pacing ever is of good pacing is the game civilization. It's famous for that one more turn feeling. So the game loop starts with just you have a unit and on your first turn you're going to choose where to build. So you've got one decision. Second turn your building is built. Your unit is free. Okay. Well now I have a unit and a building. So these are two actions that I can take. So my unit where do I move my unit? My building. What do I make in my building? All right. New turn. my building has built its thing. My unit has moved. and so on. And bit by bit, your game loop gets bigger with more actions and you feel a huge sense of progress. And the coolest thing about it is by the time you get to the end of your game loop, you already feel like playing again those things that were in the beginning of your game loop.

**Для Напарника:** Чередование Фокус -> отдых на острове; каждая следующая сессия добавляет чуть больше действий, а не сразу всё.


### Простая ядровая петля; убирай всё, что её не поддерживает  ·  [06:45](https://youtu.be/TzSp47iEFh8?t=405)
**Мысль:** Определи самое частое действие игрока и поддерживай его; дизайнер за две сессии вырезал лишнее, и игра стала весёлой и понятной.

> It can be really simple. Like I've heard a lot of people talk about in the video game space about how like a lot of side sidescrollers they see jumping as their main game loop. Like they see just that how do we make moving around in the game fun cuz that's the thing that players are going to be doing most of they're going to be running around the world. And so I think in board games similarly identify those things that players are going to be doing a lot of and make sure that those things are being supported by the game. Like what happened with a designer who came to me with a game that he wasn't sure if it was fun. We identified the core game loop. We did two sessions where we just removed stuff that wasn't supporting the core game loop. And in two sessions, he went from not being sure if it's fun to absolutely the game being fun, testing really well, and it literally getting to a place where it's currently being pitched and the designer is like really proud of it. That can happen when you identify the game's loop. Take out stuff that doesn't support it. And in so many cases, that just immediately frees up the game. It makes it makes it really clear to players what they're doing.

**Для Напарника:** Ядро Напарника: шаг -> Фокус -> рост острова. Любую новую фичу сверять: поддерживает ли она это ядро; иначе убирать.



# 🎬 Who Gets to be Awesome in Games? — Game Maker's Toolkit


### Подарить фантазию сразу: простые вводы — эффектный результат  ·  [00:25](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=25)
**Мысль:** Batman Arkham и Spider-Man переводят простые намерения в красивые результаты: щадящие системы, подсказки, магнитное наведение. Спам кнопки выглядит круто, а не наказуем.

> And from my experience of playing this sort of game, it feels like there's two, distinctly different ways of delivering this fantasy. The first way is to simply give the fantasy to the player. Easily and immediately. Which is something we see in a number of recent super hero games: in the Batman Arkham games, the free-flow combat system makes it effortless to beat up baddies. And in Spider-Man, we don't need a lengthy tutorial to swing around the city with ease and grace. This is usually achieved by providing the sensation of being powerful, which happens through simple inputs that get translated into big, flashy animations. Cinematic set-pieces with pop-up buttons. Lenient systems that subtly fix your mistakes. Magnetic snapping towards enemies. Big, obvious indicators that tell you when to counter. Dopey AI that waits patiently to attack. And so on. These games work really hard to make you feel awesome, by translating your simple intentions into impressive feats on screen. Troy Skinner, producer at Batman-publisher WB Games, explains it like this: TROY SKINNER: When people come in they spam the punch-in-the-face button. In a lot of games you get punished for that. It makes you look bad. You fail. In this game you spam the punch-in-the-face button and you look awesome! You are in fact Batman effectively punching people in the face".

**Для Напарника:** В Фокусе и Доме любое простое действие (один тап «начать») должно давать заметный тёплый отклик, а не ошибку или штраф.


### Заслуженная фантазия силы слаще подаренной  ·  [01:50](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=110)
**Мысль:** DOOM Eternal требует мастерства; создатель сравнивает это с подъёмом на гору против лифта. Преодоление делает награду ценнее.

> Through skill and mastery. Which is the approach of this year's DOOM Eternal. That game has a very stiff learning curve with all sorts of mechanics and systems to figure out - including resource management, enemy weak points, weapon switching, lighting-fast movement, the flame belch, the chainsaw, the blood punch, and more. And until you master all of them, you're going to fail - and you certainly won't feel like the bad-ass demon slayer in the game's marketing. Which means that the opportunity to live out the power fantasy is withheld until you've proven that you have real skill and mastery over a complex set of mechanics. Hugo Martin, is the creative director on DOOM Eternal, and explains it like so: HUGO MARTIN: "Hey man I'm dying to these characters because I'm not doing certain things. I don't feel strong. It's like yeah: but you're gonna feel strong when you master how to beat them". So we can give the fantasy to the player, or we can make them earn it. But why would a developer choose one over the other? Well, Hugo Martin puts it very succinctly when he says: HUGO MARTIN: "We wanna give you something to master. Because the power fantasy that is earned is far more satisfying than the one that is just handed to you". By making the player actually overcome challenge, failure, and frustration, the end result will be way more rewarding. He compares the experience of mastering DOOM Eternal to that of climbing a mountain - it's much more satisfying, he says, to climb it yourself than to simply take an elevator to the top.

**Для Напарника:** Часть наград острова оставить за редкими находками и серией усилий, чтобы они ощущались заслуженными.


### Дилемма: доступность против глубины  ·  [05:15](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=315)
**Мысль:** Лёгкая фантазия доступна всем, но кажется поверхностной опытным; заслуженная удовлетворяет, но закрывает дверь многим.

> Ultimately, then, we end up with a conundrum. If we make the power fantasy easily attainable, the game is accessible to all but can end up feeling shallow to more seasoned players. And if we force the player to earn the fantasy, the experience might be significantly more satisfying - but we lock out a considerable number of people. By making one type of player feel awesome, we lose the other.

**Для Напарника:** Для Напарника важно давать и мгновенный уют, и более глубокий слой для тех, кто хочет усложнять.


### Опции игрового процесса важнее ползунка сложности  ·  [05:49](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=349)
**Мысль:** Сложность не уменьшает число кнопок и сложность управления. Ассисты (тормоза, автопереключение, линия трассы в Forza) снижают требуемую точность и независимы от силы соперников.

> The first solution is to provide options. But not the type you might be thinking about. Because, yes, difficulty options will allow players to tune the game to their ability level. But they rarely fix the actual issue: the inherent complexity of the game. Making DOOM Eternal easier will let you make a few more mistakes, but it doesn't reduce the number of buttons you need to remember - and making Spider-Man harder doesn't actually change the web-swinging at all. So more important are gameplay and accessibility options. And for a strong example, take Forza Horizon 4. Here, you can enable things like assisted brakes and steering, automated gear shifting, and a racing line. With these options turned on, the game helps players reach the fantasy of being a racing driver - by reducing the need for precision, limiting the number of things you need to juggle, and providing lots of information. With these options, you can essentially switch the game from being a rather hardcore simulation racer to a fun and casual arcade romp. And best of all, this is all independent from the difficulty level of your rival racers - so you can reduce the complexity of controlling the car, but without also dumbing down the competition. Other examples of gameplay options include the automatic combo mode in Devil May Cry, the way Jedi Fallen Order makes parry timing windows bigger on easy mode, and how some of the Arkham games let you turn off those counter indicators so you have to pay attention to the enemy animations.

**Для Напарника:** Дать независимые настройки помощи (напоминания, подсказки следующего шага), а не только «лёгкий/сложный» режим.


### Низкий порог входа и высокий потолок мастерства  ·  [07:20](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=440)
**Мысль:** В Bayonetta можно жать кнопки и выглядеть круто, но скрытая глубина даёт простор для мастерства. Skill floor и skill ceiling — разные оси.

> A second solution is to reward mastery - but not actually require it. For this one, let's look at Bayonetta. This is a game where you almost immediately feel empowered and awesome: thanks to great animation, absurd finishing moves, a very forgiving dodge, and a reasonably low level of challenge. You can basically just mash the controller against your face and Bayonetta will pull off a huge variety of awesome-looking attacks. It's easy to feel fluid, cool, and competent in this game. But there's so much hidden mastery in this game for those who are willing to put in the work. Things like the dodge offset system, and the huge number of possible moves - means that those who want to get combo mad can express their skill and mastery. That ends up being the real power fantasy which is, indeed, more satisfying… because it's earned. If we think about this by borrowing terms from e-sports: there's skill floor, which describes the base-line level of skill needed to be effective with that character. And skill ceiling, which describes the highest level of skill you can potentially express. If Spider-Man has a low skill floor, but low skill ceiling - and DOOM Eternal has a high skill ceiling and a high skill floor to match, something like Bayonetta strikes a better balance by having a low skill floor and a high skill ceiling.

**Для Напарника:** Награды острова доступны любому, а редкие находки и глубокие комбинации поощряют тех, кто вовлечён больше.


### Наращивай сложность постепенно  ·  [08:38](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=518)
**Мысль:** В Ori игра начинается с прыжка и движения и открывает приёмы по ходу, растягивая кривую обучения на всю игру, не перегружая новичка.

> Solution three is to layer on complexity over time. In a lot of Metroidvanias, such as the Ori games, you start the game with a very simple set of controls. You can really just focus on movement and jumping, so players who are unfamiliar with platformers only need to wrestle with the absolute basics at this point. As you play on, the game gives you upgrades - like additional jumps, new attacks, more complex moves, and so on. By the end of the game, Ori has become a complex game with a huge moveset: but without overwhelming anyone in the process. By stretching the learning curve out to encompass pretty much the entire game, players can gradually get used to more and more complex gameplay. We do see some of this in DOOM Eternal, with weapons and enemy types being introduced throughout the campaign. And in Spider-Man, where the skill tree lets you add new mechanics that let you swing faster - but also increase the complexity of the controls.

**Для Напарника:** Открывать функции Напарника (разбиение задачи, очередь шагов, мир) постепенно по мере использования, а не всё сразу.


### Несколько путей к успеху: навык или время и усилие  ·  [10:42](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=642)
**Мысль:** В Hades можно побеждать, подбирая удобное оружие или просто накапливая постоянные улучшения за долгую игру.

> And finally, solution five, is to provide multiple ways to win. I've been playing a lot of Hades lately. And in each run of this mythological roguelite, you pick a weapon - perhaps a sword, or maybe a bow and arrow.
