Some of these weapons are far easier to use than others: the shield, for example, lets you absorb enemy attacks and play in a very defensive and reactionary manner. Whereas the fists force you to get up close and personal with your enemies, which puts you at a significant risk. By having different ways to succeed, players can gravitate towards the weapon, the character, or the build - that works for them. Hades also lets you unlock additional power, health, and help - so it's possible to succeed not by getting better - but by simply playing the game for a long time. That's not my personal preference for how roguelikes should work: but I can see the advantage in giving players a different way to succeed. Through time and effort, rather than skill and mastery.

**Для Напарника:** Прогресс острова растёт просто от присутствия и регулярных сессий, независимо от «качества» фокуса, давая путь для тех, кому тяжело концентрироваться.


### Фантазия силы подвижна: растёт вместе с игроком  ·  [11:39](https://youtu.be/XSvclSkmdyY?t=699)
**Мысль:** Помощники снижают XP, чтобы со временем их отключали; награды за новое оружие в Hades; сложность в Ori растёт сама. Каждый уровень навыка должен ощущаться сильным, но с ростом.

> Most of these solutions are about the same exact thing: letting players find their own way to feel powerful. That might mean using accessibility options and tuning down the difficulty, or it might mean ramping the game up to hard, playing with the most challenging character, and exploring the game's most complex rules. Both of those might be the optimum challenge for two completely different people. And something else that a lot of these solutions have in common is that they encourage players to always be pushing themselves to be better. To be moving towards more challenging and complex gameplay. In Forza, using those assist options reduces the amount of XP you gain, so you're encouraged to eventually turn them off as you get better at the game. In Bayonetta, scrappy play leads to rubbish trophies, so you're motivated to be more thoughtful with your actions. In Hades, there are rewards for trying out weapons you haven't used in a while. And in Ori, the game just naturally gets more complex, the further you go. Because the power fantasy, it turns out, is not one specific point on the learning curve: it's something more dynamic, that can move and grow as you do. You might feel the power fantasy immediately, and then feel it even more strongly as you challenge yourself to do better, to explore more of the game's systems, and to increase the challenge of the game. So ultimately, if the question is: who gets to be powerful? My answer would be: everyone. Not just those who are skilful. And not just those who want to feel immediate empowerment. But games should use design and systems to ensure that at every skill level, the player feels powerful and masterful. But always with room to grow and improve.

**Для Напарника:** Мягко стимулировать рост (например, предлагать длиннее сессию), не наказывая за короткие.



# 🎬 Why Are Horror Games So Cozy? — Mikey Lee


### Моменты покоя усиливают напряжение и успокаивают тело  ·  [02:04](https://youtu.be/IyLy-HUQMRo?t=124)
**Мысль:** В Resident Evil 2 комната сохранения светлая, музыка идёт в темпе покоя сердца и физически замедляет игрока. Контраст делает и страх, и безопасность сильнее.

> Horror games in general are designed to keep you on edge for long periods, but game designers have figured out that in order for scares to hit harder, they also need to give you moments of peace, too. In every game in the genre, there's always a moment where things just kind of stop. There will always be a brief moment where there are no enemies and the music shifts from audio violence to something more calming, even if it's nothing at all. And these are the moments where you finally get to breathe again. I'm not even joking. This made me jump while I was editing it. For me, the best example of this is the dark room in Resident Evil 2. After arriving at the RPD, fighting the Licker and hordes of zombies, you eventually arrive at that door over there. And when you open it and walk in, the entire mood of the game changes. This isn't your first save point, but it is the one that compounds the chill factor. It's brightly lit, the tension disappears, and you'd never know there was an outbreak outside if you only saw this room. And then the music kicks in. And it's not just a bop, either. It's literally designed to chill you out. The tempo sits loosely at a resting heart rate, and the intention from the devs was to give you a secure place, which is the name of the song, as well, to physically slow back down again. And it really works because the second you hear that song, you feel safe.

**Для Напарника:** Звук костра и очага Напарника как «безопасное место»: темп и тембр около темпа покоя, чтобы тело расслаблялось.


### Безопасные места сбрасывают ритм и дают смелость идти вперёд  ·  [04:36](https://youtu.be/IyLy-HUQMRo?t=276)
**Мысль:** Ритм напряжения и разрядки, где безопасные точки (Firelink Shrine) дают разрешение выдохнуть; знание об их существовании толкает вперёд через страшное.

> And this emotional contrast is the whole point. If a horror game is an emotional roller coaster, then these top parts are boss fights, jump scares, chase sequences, and these bottom parts are lo-fi chill beats, cup of coffee, and a pan of chocolate. And by the way, if you scoop up all of the songs from horror games in those little low points, those little troughs, and put them on one playlist, you'll have made an absolute banger of a mixtape. I've linked mine in the description below if you want to check it out. It also serves to create this addictive emotional rhythm, tension and release, fear, safety, chaos, control. And the safe spaces are where the rhythm resets. It's the admin office in Amnesia: The Bunker in between bouts of hiding from an unkillable beast. It's the Firelink Shrine in Dark Souls after cheesing your way through 100 enemies after initially dying 200 times. It's the apartment in Silent Hill 4 after mercilessly beating your worm in the subway until the room gets haunted, cuz then fuck that. When you've been under threat for an hour, these spaces give you permission to breathe for a sec. They're like small mercies scattered throughout games that are usually devoid of it. Knowing these spaces exist propels you forward through the terrifying parts because you know you'll have somewhere to take a break eventually. And if you can't handle pressing forward, you can always fall back to this one.

**Для Напарника:** Дом и очаг в AppBackdrop — безопасное место, куда всегда можно вернуться после неудачной сессии, без стыда.


### Маленькие моменты контроля делают игру уютной  ·  [08:53](https://youtu.be/IyLy-HUQMRo?t=533)
**Мысль:** Инвентарь, ключ ко всем дверям, шорткаты, управление ресурсами дают чувство контроля; чем их больше, тем меньше интенсивность. Облегчение после стресса — сильнейший дофаминовый удар.

> What's funny is that horror games have more in common with cozy games than you might think. Like how is the Resi 4 inventory screen not a cozy game? You pick this guy up, you put him over there, give this little chap a choice, pop him in there. Very nice. Very evil. What I'm getting at is that a big part of horror games is chaos, but within that chaos are these little moments of control. >> >> And the more they grow, the less intense the game becomes. It's like that feeling of trying to open 68,000 doors, and they're all locked before you finally find a key that unlocks them all. Or when you finally complete a puzzle that initially made zero sense. Or that Metroidvania feeling of when you unlock a shortcut at the end of an area that loops back to the beginning and saves you tons of time and aggro. But By the way, I know that some people are going to say that Dark Souls or Elden Ring or Demon's Souls aren't horror games, but when you've got an enemy like the finger creeper in it or those crow things from Bloodborne, your argument becomes null and void. All of these are just more ways the game surrenders a bit more control to you. It also exists in careful resource management, which doesn't sound sexy, but the delayed gratification of finally pulling out your magnum dong to use on a boss is absolutely unmatched. And when things are chaotic, like tackling an arena full of nightmares, the unbridled joy of running around hoovering up the spoils of war knowing there are no more threats in the area is a fitting reward for the stress. There are very few games that dish out dopamine hits like a horror game does because the stakes in other games are so rarely so stacked against you. There's no better feeling than going from piss your pants scared to I am so over-prepared for this area that I will now laugh in the face of every enemy I meet.

**Для Напарника:** Давать пользователю ощутимые точки контроля (закрыть шаг, переставить очередь) и контраст «стало получаться» после сложной сессии.


### Уют = пространство, время, контроль  ·  [11:42](https://youtu.be/IyLy-HUQMRo?t=702)
**Мысль:** Три условия комфорта: психологическое пространство без постоянной угрозы, свобода от срочности (возможность подумать и спланировать, пауза) и контроль (понимать и влиять).

> I think for horror to be comfortable, it needs to give you a few things. Number one, space. Yes, in a physical sense, you know, distance from the monsters, but more in a psychological way. Like, can I vibe without immediate threat from the monsters that allows me time to have a little mooch around or click on a specific desk 50 times for a secret message. Number two, time. Not literal time, more like freedom from urgency. Time to think about where you need to go next, plan your route, and pause mentally. This one doesn't just apply to safe spaces, either. It also applies to bringing up your map or your inventory screen. You know, can I bring up my map so I know where I'm going next, or can I bring up my inventory and eat a herb in the middle of a fight? Like, "Oh, hold on a sec." >> >> I'm really bad. Finally, number three, control. And this is a biggie, but it's a simple one. Can you gain an understanding and influence what's happening? I don't expect all three things at once, necessarily, but as long as they can grant you those three wishes over time, like some kind of fucked up zombie genie, it's pretty easy to carve out your own comfy little spot.

**Для Напарника:** Проверочный список для экранов Напарника: нет срочности, можно поставить на паузу без наказания, человек понимает, что происходит, и влияет на это.



# 🎬 Why Cause and Effect is Vital for Great Game Design — Madbook


### Причина и следствие: человек применяет законы реального мира к виртуальному  ·  [01:25](https://youtu.be/SdJo-AGN4SQ?t=85)
**Мысль:** Если правила мира нельзя вывести, игрок расстраивается и уходит. Нужна логическая линия от причины к следствию и достаточно заметный эффект.

> We as humans live in a physical world with a natural set of rules things just don't spontaneously happen at least as far as our everyday life is concerned when we are presented with a virtual world we automatically try to apply the rules of our physical world to the virtual one if the real world rules don't work we try and work out the rules for the new virtual world if we are unable to work out how the rules of the world work we get frustrated and give up at each stage of this workflow a person is constantly trying to apply the principle of cause and effect to try and understand what's going on the person's inputs are the causes when they try and make things happen and then the resulting things that happen are the effects the outcomes and this is true whether you're in a real world or in a virtual world like a game for it to make sense and feel good there needs to be a logical line from cause to effect throughout the whole flow of events and a significant enough effect that the player can really easily see what's going on.

**Для Напарника:** Каждое действие в Напарнике должно иметь очевидный видимый результат: нажал Старт -> кот устроился рядом; закончил -> остров вырос.


### Мгновенное исчезновение сбивает: переходы состояний объясняют, что происходит  ·  [02:31](https://youtu.be/SdJo-AGN4SQ?t=151)
**Мысль:** Враг, исчезающий мгновенно, нелогичен; взрыв, обращение в пыль или оставшийся обломок помогают понять происходящее сразу.

> For example i see a lot of beginners will make their enemies in their game just disappear when they run out of health something like this can be incredibly jarring to a player as it doesn't seem totally logical for something to just completely and instantaneously disappear looking at other games for examples they will often use an explosion or a turn to dust effect or even just leave a dead or broken version of whatever it was behind it's this persistent or the transitions from one state to another that allows the player to understand what is happening as it's happening instead of retroactively trying to work out what just happened.

**Для Напарника:** Появление и исчезновение элементов острова и шагов делать плавным переходом, а не скачком.


### Шахматы: контроль игрока и ясные причина-следствие без лишних эффектов  ·  [06:09](https://youtu.be/SdJo-AGN4SQ?t=369)
**Мысль:** Игрок сам берёт и переносит фигуру, клик показывает возможные ходы, постановка сопровождается приятным звуком. Игрок никогда не гадает, что делать дальше.

> However in addition to the sound effect the fact that the player virtually picks up the piece and drags it all the way across the board ensures they are in control of the actions that are happening at all times and can mean that they have a perfect understanding of the flow of events in their head so even in this incredibly simple example we have two entire cause and effect scenarios you click your mouse the cause it picks up the piece and shows you where it can move keeping the player informed a visual effect you place your piece because the game plays a really satisfying sound effect to show that you've successfully played a piece effect at no point in this entire scenario is the player left to wonder what they have to do next at least in terms of using the game's controls everything they do to cause an impact on the game by moving pieces has a really obvious and logical effect.

**Для Напарника:** Старт шага: нажатие показывает, что дальше, подтверждается мягким звуком, без вопросов и лишних эффектов.



# 🎬 Why Cozy Games Aren't "Real" Games — Juniper Dev


### Уютные игры расширяют определение хорошей игры  ·  [00:13](https://youtu.be/2m2laj1IM1g?t=13)
**Мысль:** Без давления, угроз и дедлайнов, с узнаваемым повторением и возможностью отключить мозг; формально «плохая» игра оказывается очень успешной.

> Cozy games are meant to lessen your anxiety there's no pressure no threats no deadlines no expectations but these cozy games go against everything that a video game should be the whole appeal of video games are to make you think in some way or have intensity some sort of action maybe you're given a challenge and you struggle until you get better maybe it's even competitive but with this newer genre of games almost none of that is wanted in fact a big appeal is the almost idleness of them the comfort and familiar repetition the lack of stress because there aren't high stakes the ability to turn off your brain and just play by every connotation of what a video game should be cozy games are bad games but they're not because they're extremely successful so what they're doing instead is just expanding our definition of what makes a good game good.

**Для Напарника:** Легитимирует тон Напарника: отсутствие давления и дедлайнов — не слабость продукта, а источник его ценности.


### Общее для всех игр — ощущение достижения и прогресса  ·  [01:11](https://youtu.be/2m2laj1IM1g?t=71)
**Мысль:** Во всех жанрах игры дают иллюзию достижения и прогресса, и это ощущение приносит радость, как и в реальной жизни.

> Think about a video game where you win for a team you explore a really big world you powerwash that deck you kill everyone you slowly get better at that really hard level those are all parts of such different games but what do all those experiences have in common they give you the sense of accomplishment and progress that is the one thing that's Universal to all games whether it's a shooter an MMO a puzzle game or a farming simulator you could say that all video games do are give you the illusion of accomplishment and progress because it's not anything real but you do feel it and that feeling brings you Joy and isn't that why we pursue accomplishment and progress in the real world too obviously we also do it to help other people and make the world better but at a personal level it's nothing more than seeking purpose and happiness.

**Для Напарника:** Основной продуктовый ход Напарника — ощутимое ощущение «я продвинулся» после каждой сессии.


### Грань между уютом и скукой тонка  ·  [08:04](https://youtu.be/2m2laj1IM1g?t=484)
**Мысль:** Нужно расслабление, но не рутина; без спешки, но с целями; головоломки удовлетворяют, но не нагружают; мир увлекателен, но не слишком велик.

> That's because the line of cozy game and boring game is very thin cuz this is what cozy games have to do the player should be able to relax in mine or tend to their Farm but it can't feel too grindy they shouldn't feel time bound or rushed but they should still have goals and be encouraged to do things puzzles shouldn't require too much thought but they should still be satisfying the world should be fun to explore but it can't be too big or else it's overwhelming from a developer perspective think of how hard that is it's no wonder why so many of these games are coming out and why so many of them just aren't the same as something like stardew Valley because stardo Valley struck a balance that is so hard to replicate.

**Для Напарника:** Проверять Дом и Мир: достаточно, чтобы было чем заняться, но без перегруза и без пустоты.


### Stardew: самонаправляемость при очень низком давлении  ·  [08:52](https://youtu.be/2m2laj1IM1g?t=532)
**Мысль:** Нет лимитов времени и прямых квестов, лишь расплывчатые подсказки; выбор активности каждый день, а усердие определяет, сколько получишь. Базовое давление низкое, поэтому каждый настраивает свой уют.

> The game starts with a cut scene showing you how your character is tired of being a corporate slave and is given their grandfather's Old Farm it's in bad shape though so it becomes your mission to clean things up and well your dead grandfather will visit you again in 2 years to check up on things and how you go about doing that is completely up to you slowly unlock different activities and are given vague indicators of what you should do but there are no time limits no direct quests aside from the occasional low pressure task each day you choose what you want to do and slowly learn more about the backstory of each character and the nearby Village it's extremely self-guided and selfmotivated which is why so many people love it if you want to be like me and max out the community center as fast as humanly possible you can do that the level of rigor you put into the game is what you get out of it and because the base line of pressure is very low everyone can create their own balance of coziness.

**Для Напарника:** Давать Фокусу и Миру выбор интенсивности самим человеком, без обязательных квестов и дедлайнов.


### Без адреналина нужна другая глубина  ·  [09:41](https://youtu.be/2m2laj1IM1g?t=581)
**Мысль:** Уютные игры проваливаются, когда потолок уюта низок и задачи расплывчаты. Взамен адреналина нужны механики, делающие повторение приятным, разнообразие, насыщенный мир, кастомизация и видимый прогресс с уважением ко времени.

> The Cozy games that fall short usually do it because that cozy maximum is too low there isn't enough to do or tasks are too vague so the average player gets bored I imagine that it's very tempting as a developer to see a genre defined by relaxation and low stress and think that it's going to be an easy game to make cuz in theory you should be able to get away with less content more repetitive tasks and you're not tasked with creating adrenaline in the player or making an experience hard but in the absence of those things there's more that cozy games need to have instead mechanics that make repetition satisfying and a wide variety of them a rich world that feels immersive heavy personalization elements and above all else you still have to do the one thing we talked about earlier that all video games have in common show you that you've made progress and respect your time enough as a player to make it worth it.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-лист для острова: приятное повторение, разнообразие, персонализация, всегда видимый прогресс и уважение ко времени.



# 🎬 Why Cozy Games Keep Disappointing Us - And Who's Actually Getting It Right — JaeTeeGames


### Уют рождается из освоения последствий, а не из их отсутствия  ·  [01:16](https://youtu.be/Pu6dw07JEo4?t=76)
**Мысль:** Уютное чувство в Harvest Moon шло от порядка, наведённого над хаосом: сорняки превращаются в ферму. Дофамин идёт от самой работы, а не от отсутствия трения.

> Cozy wasn't always a marketing tag. It was a byproduct of hard work. If we go back to the late '9s to the era of Harvest Moon on the SNES or the N64, nobody used the word cozy. We called these farming sims or management RPGs. And if you actually go back and play Harvest Moon 64, it is stressful. You had a stamina bar that depleted rapidly. If you work too hard, you passed out. If you didn't water your crops, they died. And if you didn't feed your cows, they got sick. The cozy feeling didn't come from the lack of consequences. It came from the mastery of those consequences. It was a satisfaction of order over chaos. You took a field of weeds and rocks, which is stressful, and you turned it into a profitable farm, which is relaxing.

**Для Напарника:** Остров должен расти за счёт реальных усилий сессий (победа над хаосом задачи), а не выдаваться даром; но без наказаний за пропуск.


### Индустрия скопировала эстетику Stardew, а не механику  ·  [02:46](https://youtu.be/Pu6dw07JEo4?t=166)
**Мысль:** Клоны сняли трение и глубину, оставив пиксель-арт и пастель. Эстетику легко подделать в трейлере, а ценность даёт петля.

> The industry saw the success of Stardew Valley, but they learned the wrong lesson. They didn't look at the mechanics. They looked at the aesthetic. They saw pixel art plus farming equals millions of dollars. And so the cozy clone rush began. Developers started stripping away the friction, stripping away the difficulty, and stripping away the debt, thinking if they made it just cuter, it would sell.

**Для Напарника:** Не полагаться на милоту кота и острова: за красивой оболочкой должна стоять работающая петля Фокус -> рост острова.


### Простое не равно бездумное: трение без награды - это нудно  ·  [05:10](https://youtu.be/Pu6dw07JEo4?t=310)
**Мысль:** Простая мини-игра должна иметь кривую навыка и ощущение роста. Если красиво, но тратит время впустую, это уже не уют.

> There is a difference between simple and mindless. Dardu Valley's fishing miniame is simple, but it requires focus and has a skill curve. You get better at it. Mano's crafting felt like a chore I had to do to unlock the next cutscene. It was friction without reward. If a game respects your eyes but disrespects your time, it isn't cozy. It's tedious.

**Для Напарника:** Действия на острове и в сессии должны давать ощущение мастерства или прогресса, а не быть механической повинностью.


### Уют живёт в тактильности действия, а не в цвете  ·  [10:17](https://youtu.be/Pu6dw07JEo4?t=617)
**Мысль:** В Tiny Glade нет цели, но заборы защёлкиваются, окна вырастают, тропинка огибает дерево. Месяцы труда ушли на то, чтобы клик ощущался сочно.

> Or look at Tiny Glade. This is a game with no combat, no farming, and no management. It is purely aesthetic, but it passes the loop test. Why? Because the mechanic of building feels tactile. The way the fences snap together, the way the windows pop into existence, the way the path logically curves around a tree. The cozy comes from the code, not just the colors. The developer spent months making the mouse click feel juicy.

**Для Напарника:** Каждое касание на острове (посадка, появление постройки) стоит довести до тактильного отклика: анимация плюс хаптика на событие.



# 🎬 Why do cozy games suck? — koramora


### Риск — это и время; награда должна оправдывать потраченное  ·  [03:44](https://youtu.be/MnMQgN0aTQM?t=224)
**Мысль:** Даже уютной игре нужен баланс «вложение — отдача»: зачем рисковать часом, если ничего не получишь.

> Games don't have to have combat to have a good risk reward system the risk could just be the time you're putting into the game is the reward from completing this goal worth the time that you have to put into it like the catch 100 fish in a Nook mile goal is it worth your time it's just a few Nook miles you can get these by spending more time completing the easier Nook mile plus goals but for some people the reward of completing the goal is worth the risk of spending time to catch 99 fish in a row messing up that last one and then having to restart the entire fishing process all over again a lot of cozy games don't have risk which makes sense right they're supposed to be cozy but we need to be rewarded somehow for our work why risk an hour of my time to get nothing out of it I need to work towards something.

**Для Напарника:** Каждая сессия должна приносить ощутимое: рост острова, находка, реплика; без риска потери и наказания.


### Прогресс ради прогресса не держит; нужен мир, который важен  ·  [04:31](https://youtu.be/MnMQgN0aTQM?t=271)
**Мысль:** Без интересной истории или награды за исследование нет причины возвращаться.

> Games in general need some type of progression whether that's unlocking items to decorate with or completing a full-blown story there has to be some kind of satisfaction when playing and the story better be good there are some cozy games that you progress through a story but sometimes that story just isn't captivating I don't like progressing just for the sake of progressing unless I care about the world and the characters or I'm rewarded for exploring the world and if the story progression isn't appealing enough then the game is Bor boring and it doesn't give me a reason to keep coming back.

**Для Напарника:** Остров и кот должны давать причину вернуться через мир и персонажа, а не одну полоску прогресса.


### Долго ≠ интересно: игроки хотят, чтобы время стоило того  ·  [06:02](https://youtu.be/MnMQgN0aTQM?t=362)
**Мысль:** Затягивание длительности без содержания лишь повышает шанс бросить игру.

> A lot of these cozy games make players do stuff for the sake of doing stuff why am I playing this game is it just to kill time or do I want it to be engaging just because a part of a game takes a long time to complete doesn't replace the fact that you need to add content stuff taking a long time doesn't equal more engaging content it just gives players more of a reason to fall out of a game that content better be appealing especially if it's a mechanic that requires a lot of time taken from someone's day players want to play games that are worth their time not a waste of time.

**Для Напарника:** Не растягивать прогресс острова искусственно; ценить время человека с СДВГ.


### Без капли сложности скучно: игра не должна играть сама  ·  [10:56](https://youtu.be/MnMQgN0aTQM?t=656)
**Мысль:** Полное устранение трудностей отнимает интерес.

> Players don't want games that just play themselves I still need some level of tediousness to keep me interested I don't want to avoid everything difficult in a game don't let me just run around the trainer there still needs to be some type of challenge.

**Для Напарника:** Лёгкая сложность в Фокусе (мини-цели, выбор длительности) — не всё должно быть автоматическим.



# 🎬 Why Does Celeste Feel So Good to Play? — Game Maker's Toolkit


### Мелкая короткая обратная связь делает движение ощутимым  ·  [08:40](https://youtu.be/yorTG9at90g?t=520)
**Мысль:** Сжатие/растяжение, пыль при приземлении, вибрация, микропауза и тень: тонкие эффекты вместо «сока» на каждом шагу.

> Not just the numbers that makes Madeline’s moveset feels so good: art, animation, and sound effects help sell the feeling, too. Like the squash and squeeze on her jump, which really emphasises the movement in her body. The way tiny dust particles kick up, and the controller rumbles, when you hit the ground, to sell the impact. The four-frame pause and microscopic screen shake whenever you dash, which makes the jolting movement feel even more heightened. And the trail of shadows and the blazing white contrail, which emphasise the speed and direction. Celeste isn’t a bonkers Vlambeer-style juice fest, but these subtle and short-lived effects do a lot of heavy lifting in making the game feel better - and giving feedback to your inputs and actions.

**Для Напарника:** Хаптика и микроэффекты на событиях мира (завершение сессии, находка), короткие и тонкие.


### Прощающие механики: coyote time, буфер ввода — работа с намерением игрока  ·  [09:20](https://youtu.be/yorTG9at90g?t=560)
**Мысль:** Игра прощает мелкие промахи в кадрах: прыжок после схода с платформы, буфер нажатия; промах игрока ощущается честно.

> While Celeste is a very hard game at times - and gets even harder if you go for the strawberries, and cassette tapes, and B-Side levels, and so on, the developers are actually extremely generous to the player, with an untold number of hacks and special cases in the code that make the game way less punishing. The most famous, perhaps, is the way you can jump a few frames after leaving a platform - and still spring into the air. This is called Coyote time, named after the bits in Loony Toon cartoons where characters run off platforms and keep sprinting in mid-air. But there are loads of others hidden in the code. Dashing into a corner won’t bonk you into oblivion, but you’ll merely sail around the edge of the platform. Spikes have a small hit box, so you won’t die if you touch the very tip. These traffic light blocks will still grant you momentum, even if you’re a little late on the button press. If you do a climb jump but quickly push away from the wall, the game switches to a wall jump and refunds the stamina. And if you jump just before touching the ground, the game will remember that and execute the command when you land. NOEL: Because it feels bad if you press jump the frame before you touch the ground and just touch the ground and there’s nothing. You want it to feel like "no, I pressed the jump button, I was right there...". It feels like the game messed up, like the game missed your input or something - it got eaten. And you don’t want that, you want to feel like you’re in control of your character and it’s doing what you want it to do. And when you do mess up it’s more on the player figuring out like “how do I solve this problem and execute it better" and not "I was one frame off and the game's has killed me". MATT: It’s like working on the player’s intent rather than making it a precise simulation of pressing buttons at the correct time.

**Для Напарника:** Прощающий дизайн Напарника: продолжить сессию после перерыва, засчитать почти-старт; «не успел» не наказывается.


### Прощение для новичков, глубина для профи — одно и то же правило  ·  [11:12](https://youtu.be/yorTG9at90g?t=672)
**Мысль:** Допуск в кадрах делает игру приятнее казуалам, а мастера используют его для техники.

> And the cool thing too about that is for actually really pro players, people who get really good at it, it does come down to those frames. Even if you say "you get three frames off of a ledge, and you can still jump" - for a normal player that just makes the game feel better. But then for pro players who actually are trying to get through each level super fast, or trying to execute something perfectly, then they get down to the frames, like "no, I have three extra frames so I can use those to get across a gap I’m not supposed to get across" MATT: They abuse it NOEL: Yeah. So you still get that frame perfect play that’s possible but then for casual players it just feels better.

**Для Напарника:** Простые режимы для новичков с возможностью углубления: базовая сессия и расширенные опции.


### Сделай приятным сам момент взаимодействия, даже без целей  ·  [13:31](https://youtu.be/yorTG9at90g?t=811)
**Мысль:** Если персонажем весело просто управлять в пустой комнате, всё остальное строится легче.

> Just make sure that the moment to moment feels good so that when someone’s just sitting there with a controller, the room could be empty but they can move their character around, make that feel good MATT: With no goals or anything, just make sure it’s fun to be that character. I remember playing Mario Sunshine and just running around Delfino Plaza for hours beause it was just fun.

**Для Напарника:** Кота и остров приятно трогать и без задач: тап/касание отвечает тёплой реакцией.

# 🎬 Why Idle Games Feel So Good (Even While You're Asleep) 🍪 — Slimedex


### Обратная потеря: возвращение как спасательная операция, трение нулевое  ·  [03:23](https://youtu.be/U2gV5vacQyE?t=203)
**Мысль:** Если не заходить, виртуальные монеты накапливаются, и проверка превращается в получение посылки; трение игры сведено к нулю.

> Why does this feel so good? Idle games flip loss aversion in reverse. If the slime does not open the game, those imaginary coins still pile up, but the slime never sees them. >> >> So, checking in becomes a tiny rescue mission. Picture a happy gold slime running to the mailbox every morning to collect a package it did not order. The friction of playing has been shrunk to zero. Tap, collect, smile, close.

**Для Напарника:** Возвращение в Дом как получение подарка от острова/кота, без чувства долга.


### Три правила: числа растут всегда, растут в твоё отсутствие, решения редки  ·  [07:12](https://youtu.be/U2gV5vacQyE?t=432)
**Мысль:** Нет проигрышного состояния; офлайн-прогресс превращает отсутствие в подарок; ожидается взгляд на 10 секунд несколько раз в день, а не час сосредоточения.

> Rule one. Numbers go up. Always. There is no losing state in an idle game. The slime's coin pile only grows. Even if the slime is bad at the game, it is mathematically winning every single second. Rule two. Numbers go up while you are gone. Offline progress turns absence into a gift. The slime is rewarded for closing the app, which feels strange and impossible and exactly why the slime opens it again 4 hours later. Rule three, big decisions are rare, tiny check-ins are constant. The slime is never asked to focus for an hour. The slime is asked to glance for 10 seconds six times a day.

**Для Напарника:** Соответствует тону без стыда: остров не уменьшается, возвращение вознаграждается; микро check-in вместо долгих сессий.



# 🎬 Why Your Brain Wants Videos You Don't Like — Curiocos


### Предсказуемая награда лёгко отпускает, случайная держит  ·  [01:39](https://youtu.be/E455eW6RqPc?t=99)
**Мысль:** Переменное подкрепление (variable ratio) даёт стабильное упорное поведение; поэтому лента выдаёт хорошее видео не всегда, а случайно.

> The answer lies not in what you see, but in its unpredictability. You might think the most engaging feed would be a constant stream of perfect videos. But, in classic experiments, researchers found the exact opposite. Predictable rewards are surprisingly easy to walk away from. The secret to making a behavior truly persistent is to make the reward show up at random. This principle was first mapped in operant conditioning experiments where animals responded on unpredictable schedules, producing steady, persistent behavior. It's called a variable ratio schedule. A reward arrives after an unpredictable number of actions, averaging a set ratio. Think of a slot machine. Most pulls give you nothing. That's what makes the occasional, random win so compelling. >> >> This unpredictability produces a steady, high rate of response. >> >> One peer-reviewed analysis notes that variable ratio schedules can lead to steady response rates, >> >> but may make behavior less resistant to extinction than standard theory predicts, a disputed point. The feed isn't designed to give you a great video every single time. It's designed to give you one at random, which is far more effective at keeping you scrolling.

**Для Напарника:** Понимание механизма, чтобы осознанно не строить на нём ядро; редкие находки на острове — как исключение, привязанное к реальному действию человека.



# 🎬 WWDC21: Design for spatial interaction | Apple — Apple Developer


### Опираться на естественное физическое действие, а не на абстрактный список опций  ·  [13:13](https://youtu.be/-eGCO-5C69g?t=793)
**Мысль:** Вместо списка AirPlay человек просто подносит телефон к нужной колонке: интерфейс уступает физической задаче, «этот/тот» вместо навигации по меню.

> In the case of HomePods, we use spatial awareness to make the task of selecting a speaker more natural. Oftentimes, I'll be listening to some music, and I'll want to move it from my iPhone to a better speaker near me. Previously, if I wanted to play this song on a different speaker, I'd have to go into the AirPlay list. In this list, all of the other devices in my space are presented equally, whether they are near me or not. What I am really interested in, though, is this particular HomePod. With this new human-scale interaction, I can finally transfer a song to this HomePod without navigating an onscreen UI, or having to interact with the screen at all, for that matter. We provide visual feedback on the HomePod itself to show that the music is about to be transferred. The light modulates precisely with my movement, growing in intensity as I approach, and shrinking as I move away. And of course, the ultimate confirmation of the transfer is the music playing from the speaker itself. By embracing the physical action, a previously intangible experience now feels completely tangible, visceral, and instinctive. Think of ways that your own interfaces can support a natural physical motion, rather than an abstracted list of options. Try to make your experiences and designs defer to the physical task at hand.

**Для Напарника:** Дом: вместо длинных меню выбора, один жест или одна кнопка прямо у объекта (например, тап по коту или по костру начинает действие).

> Great solutions take time to build and they’re a result of iteration and prototyping. This is Loona. The iOS app offers gracefully animated scenes that combine relaxing activities, storytelling, and atmospheric sounds to help you calm down or fall asleep. Bringing this UI into a windowed experience on visionOS would have been reasonable. But listen to how the team approached it. This is Sergey Gonchar, the co-founder of Loona: We just put ourselves in the mindset, we’re going to build a lot of prototypes, because we want to find the best possible experience we can shape for this platform. Take a look at the early sketches of their scooter concept. The 2D prototypes felt dimensional, they were begging to be broken into pieces to play with. And it took time, but these sketches evolved into 3D models, which took on a life of their own, with tons of fun, incredible detail and subtle animations.

**Для Напарника:** Чек-лист антипаттернов для Напарника: внешние награды, угрозы и перегруз стимулами исключать.


### Изобилие, но не переизбыток; защита и поддержка; радость простого  ·  [01:06](https://youtu.be/GI7kxMJnFeI?t=66)
**Мысль:** Уют лежит между пустой комнатой и захламлённой; персонажи поддерживают игрока, а радость в простых механиках (фермерство, рыбалка, коллекции).

> One key aspect of coziness is abundance but not overabundance so when I started to think about this I pictured a spectrum where the axis is the number of items in a room for example on either end of the spectrum it's not very cozy either it's a completely empty room or a hoarders room both not really cozy so coziness I feel like would fall more to the center right of that Spectrum just enough in the room to make it feel warm and inviting but not too much to feel hectic or not too little so that it feels cold and isolating some other key themes highlighted by project horseshoe are protection and support basically throughout the game characters are there to support you and you don't have to play that Guardian type role the mundane is also a key feature in a lot of cozy games finding joy in the simple mechanics or ideas think stardew Valley's farming and fishing or Animal Crossings collecting.

**Для Напарника:** На экране Дома и острова держать «достаточно, но не слишком»: ровно столько элементов, чтобы было тепло, а не пусто и не суетливо.


### Внутренне вознаграждающие, необязательные, низкие ставки, рутина  ·  [03:56](https://youtu.be/GI7kxMJnFeI?t=236)
**Мысль:** Рыбалка в Stardew ради процесса, а не награды, в отличие от квестов WoW; активности необязательны, без чувства вины, рутинны и без стресса.

> But one key mechanic project horseshoe is calling intrinsically rewarding activities which is BAS basically doing something not because of the general reward you'd get for doing it but because of the process of actually doing it a good example of this is World of Warcraft questing versus stardew Valley fishing most of the time in World of Warcraft you're doing the Quest for the reward whether it's better gear experience points story progression whatever but in stard to Valley at least for me I do the fishing Min game because I like it's almost a meditative practice I'm not really doing it to increase my fishing level or get money although that isn't an option it's mostly just cuz I like it cozy game mechanics are also many times optional again avoiding that sense of responsibility where you don't feel guilty for not doing something all of the goals or activities are low stakes and non-committal and primarily many of those activities emphasize that sense of mundane we talked about earlier chopping a tree fishing planting organizing Etc the routine that brings out that sense of coziness and that ties into what a lot of cozy game narratives are like running a farm or a coffee shop organizing a store or home these sort of low impact ambient non-stressful gameplay Loops.

# 🎬 Your Willpower Isn't Failing You. Your Phone Is Designed to Win — MetaTherapy


### Forest: выращиваемое дерево как устройство обязательства; визуальный прогресс, микроцели  ·  [06:13](https://youtu.be/hgxYMN_h-SQ?t=373)
**Мысль:** Желание не убить дерево создаёт небольшую психологическую цену ухода; хорошие игровые механики (прогресс, микроцели, неприятие потери) улучшают настойчивость.

> Forest is a genuinely clever app. The concept is simple. You open the app, you set a timer, and you plant a virtual seed. As long as you stay in the app and off your phone, the seed grows into a tree. If you leave to check Instagram, the tree dies. Over time, you build a whole forest, a visual record of your focus sessions. And for every real money purchase users make, Forest partners with a nonprofit to plant actual trees in Africa. As of early 2024, over 1.6 million real trees have been planted by Forest users. Boris has over 40 million users globally and consistently ranks as a top productivity app in over 130 countries. User reviews highlight something interesting. People don't describe using it as a chore. They describe it as motivating. That I don't want to kill my tree response is a form of commitment device. You've created a small psychological cost for switching away and it works. Research on gamification for behavior change shows that well-designed game mechanics, specifically visual progress indicators, clear micro goals, and loss aversion features can meaningfully improve task persistence. Forest's design hits all three. Comprehensive long-term RCT evidence specific to forest doesn't yet exist, but the behavioral mechanisms it uses are well supported in the broader literature.

**Для Напарника:** Прямой аналог острова. Не применимо: смерть дерева — штраф и стыд; у Напарника остров только растёт.

**Для Напарника:** Планировать для Напарника, что через недели ритуал приедается, и вовремя вводить новые слои (новые существа, события).


### Три способа оживить цикл: добавить, заменить, усложнить  ·  [07:30](https://youtu.be/hvM1DhnnaV4?t=450)
**Мысль:** Добавить цикл (Hades: забег и спуск), заменить (диалог вместо боя), усложнить (дополнительный ресурс для элитных юнитов).

> Например в рогло эти хадисы есть два ярко выраженных цикла которые активируются в первые десять минут игры петля забега и петлях оба они оба связаны с получением награды только в первом случае мы копим а во втором тратим и все это нужно для достижения одной цели победы над очередным о сам и продвижению по сюжету но цикл можно не только добавить например заместить как это делают в классических rpg самый очевидный пример это избежать бой диалогом прокачав нужные навыки или выполняя особые условия так вместо действия боя награды вы получите цикл действия диалог награда самый безболезненный инвариант вдохнуть в клуб новую жизнь это усложнить или усовершенствовать его так делают в стратегиях по типу героев меча и магии когда помимо покупки существ за золото появляется возможность приобрести элитных юнитов которым нужны не только золото но и специальный ресурс теперь их поиск еще одна часть игровой петли.

**Для Напарника:** Освежать Фокус добавлением нового слоя, а не заменой основы: усложнять награду (новый ресурс), не ломая ритуал.


### Сначала шлифуй core loop на пустом прототипе  ·  [08:41](https://youtu.be/hvM1DhnnaV4?t=521)
**Мысль:** Mario 64 и Jak and Daxter обкатывали движение на пустых прототипах уровней, пока основа не стала приятной.

> Поэтому первое что делают при разработке прототипа это подготавливают именно его сначала в виде схемы а потом и ты риру я основные механики которые в нем используются они зависят от жанра но как правило это передвижение бой exploring или менеджмент важность идеально сделанной механики для карлу по подтверждает опыт именитых студий так для mario 64 от nintendo и jak and daxter от ноти дог использовали одинаковый подход главный элемент карлу по а именно передвижение они обкатывали на пустых прототипах уровней пока не добились нужного результата.

**Для Напарника:** Прежде чем наращивать остров, довести до блеска базовый тап, старт сессии и отклик.


### Мета-механики: очки, ачивки, коллекции, мини-игры  ·  [09:21](https://youtu.be/hvM1DhnnaV4?t=561)
**Мысль:** Не влияют на core loop, но поддерживают интерес и дают атмосферу: редкая ачивка делает тебя «особенным», мини-игры дарят ореол погружения.

> Не менее важным мета механики в отличие от основной петли они прежде всего отвечают целям разработчика они геймеры но при этом добавляют игре лоска и помогают дольше поддерживать интерес геймплей делая его чуть разнообразней или ставя не игровые цели собственно первые мета механики это очки которые вы могли видеть в аркадных автоматах они никак не влияют на игру но помогают поддерживать к ней интерес даже когда карл уп освоен от и до современный аналог это ачивки которые придают вашему игровому опыту дополнительные цели и удовольствие имеете ачивку которую получила пять процентов игроков значит вы один из немногих особенных более продвинутые мета механики дают внутри игровые награды также как и карлу но при этом кардинально не меняют процесс игры часто это коллекционные предметы такие как пакеты в gta вайс сити за которые дают немного денег и оружия все это вы можете получить и без их поиска поэтому такая опциональная активность просто радует но не стоит воспринимать мета механики как не важны они наращивают на ваш скелет карлу по мясо которая может фактически стать залогом атмосферности многочисленные мини-игры в red dead redemption это ничто иное как мета механики но именно они дарят прекрасный ореол погружение вы можете не заметить как провели за рыбалкой или игрой в пять пальцев полчаса но все равно останетесь довольны.

**Для Напарника:** Остров: необязательные коллекции и редкие находки как мета-слой, дающий радость без давления и без обязанности.


### Три вопроса фундамента: механики, цель, награда  ·  [11:06](https://youtu.be/hvM1DhnnaV4?t=666)
**Мысль:** Перед опциональными слоями нужно ответить, какие механики, какая цель и какая награда.

> Поэтому ему важно уделить много времени если вдруг захотите создавать свой сами себе ответьте на вопросы какие механики будут в моей игре какую цель нужно достичь и какая за это будет награда когда этот фундамент будет составлен тогда можно подумать и опциональных действий.

**Для Напарника:** Для каждой новой фичи Напарника письменно отвечать на эти три вопроса.
